\min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathcal {s} _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}}, \boldsymbol {v}} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathcal {s} _ {\boldsymbol {u}}} \frac {1}{n} \left\{\boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {1} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} + \left(\boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {d} _ {\boldsymbol {y}} \boldsymbol {z}\right) \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta}\right) + \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {d} _ {\boldsymbol {y}} \mathsf {z p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v} \right\} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(v _ {i} - \varepsilon \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {q}}\right). \tag {b.41}
$$

using cgmt and the corresponding ao takes the form

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathcal {s} _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}}, \boldsymbol {v}} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathcal {s} _ {\boldsymbol {u}}} \frac {1}{n} \left\{\left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {g} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {d} _ {\boldsymbol {y}} \boldsymbol {u} + \left\| \boldsymbol {d} _ {\boldsymbol {y}} \boldsymbol {u} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} + (\boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {d} _ {\boldsymbol {y}} z) (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta}) \right. \\ \left. + \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \mathbf {1} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v} \right\} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(v _ {i} - \varepsilon \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {q}}\right), \tag {b.42} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\pmb {g}\sim \mathsf{n}(0,\pmb{i}_n)$  and  $\pmb {h}\sim \mathsf{n}(0,\pmb{i}_d)$

given  $y_{i} = \pm 1$  are independent of  $z$  and hence  $g$ , we have  $d_{\mathbf{y}}g, d_{\mathbf{y}}z \sim \mathsf{n}(0, i_n)$  and  $\| d_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{u}\|_{\ell_2} = \| \mathbf{u}\|_{\ell_2}$ . this results in

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathcal {s} _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}}, \boldsymbol {v}} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathcal {s} _ {\boldsymbol {u}}} \frac {1}{\eta} \big \{\left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {g} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {u} + \| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} + (\boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {z}) (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta}) + \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \mathbf {1} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v} \big \} \\ + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(v _ {i} - \varepsilon \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {q}}\right). \tag {b.43} \\ \end{array}
$$

letting  $\beta \coloneqq \frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\| \pmb{u}\|_{\ell_2}$  and optimizing over direction of  $\pmb{u}$ , we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathcal {s} _ {\boldsymbol {u}}} \frac {1}{n} \left(\left\| \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {g} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {u} + \left\| \boldsymbol {u} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} + (\boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} z) (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta}) + \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {1} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}\right) \\ = \max  _ {0 \leq \beta \leq k} \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\| \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {g} + \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \boldsymbol {z} + \mathbf {1} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \boldsymbol {v} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta}, \tag {b.44} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $k \coloneqq \max_{\boldsymbol{u} \in s_{\boldsymbol{u}}} \frac{1}{\sqrt{n}} \| \boldsymbol{u} \|_{\ell_2} < k_{\boldsymbol{u}}$  by definition of  $s_{\boldsymbol{u}}$ .

plugging the latter into ao becomes

$$
\min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathcal {s} _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}}, \boldsymbol {v}} \max  _ {0 \leq \beta \leq k} \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\| \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {g} + \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \boldsymbol {z} + \mathbf {1} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \boldsymbol {v} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(v _ {i} - \varepsilon \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {q}}\right).
$$

we hereafter use the shorthand

$$
\ell (\pmb {v}, \pmb {\theta}) = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(v _ {i} - \varepsilon \left\| \pmb {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {q}}\right),
$$

for simplicity of notation. for the minimization, with respect to  $\pmb{\theta}$  and then  $\pmb{v}$ , to become easier in our later calculation we proceed by writing  $\ell(\pmb{v},\pmb{\theta})$  in terms of its conjugate with respect to  $\pmb{\theta}$ . that is,

$$
\boldsymbol {\ell} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {\theta}) = \sup _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\ell}} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {w})
$$

where  $\widetilde{\ell}(\pmb{v},\pmb{w})$  is the conjugate of  $\ell$  with respect to  $\pmb{\theta}$ . the logic behind this is that ao will then simplify to

$$
\min _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathcal {s} _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}}, \boldsymbol {v}} \max _ {0 \leq \beta \leq k, \boldsymbol {w}} \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\| \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {g} + \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \boldsymbol {z} + \mathbf {1} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \boldsymbol {v} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} + \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \widetilde {\ell} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {w})
$$

which after flipping (allowed based on the correct form of convexity/concavity of po) becomes

$$
\max  _ {0 \leq \beta \leq k, \boldsymbol {w}} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in s _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}}, \boldsymbol {v}} \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\| \left\| p _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {g} + \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \boldsymbol {z} + \mathbf {1} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \boldsymbol {v} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} p _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} + \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \widetilde {\ell} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {w}). \tag {b.46}
$$

we define the parallel and perpendicular components of  $\pmb{\theta}$  along vector  $\pmb{\mu}$  as follows:

$$
\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp} = \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\theta}, \quad \theta := \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta}, \quad \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \boldsymbol {\theta} = \theta \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}. \tag {b.47}
$$

given that  $\widetilde{\mu}$  is an eigenvector of  $\sigma$ , cf. assumption 5, we have  $\mathsf{p}_{\mu} \sigma^{1/2} \mathsf{p}_{\mu}^{\perp} = 0$  and therefore

$$
\mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} = \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} \left(\mathsf {p} _ {\mu} + \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp}\right) \boldsymbol {\theta} = \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\theta} = \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp}.
$$

similarly, since  $\pmb{\sigma}^{1 / 2}\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}} = a\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$

$$
\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} = a \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} = a \theta .
$$

rewriting the ao problem, we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {0 \leq \beta \leq k, \boldsymbol {w}} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathcal {s} _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}}, \boldsymbol {v}} \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\| \left\| \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {g} + a \theta \boldsymbol {z} + \boldsymbol {1} \left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \boldsymbol {v} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \\ + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp} + \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp} + \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta - \widetilde {\ell} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {w}). \tag {b.48} \\ \end{array}
$$

we can rewrite this as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {0 \leq \beta \leq k, \boldsymbol {w}} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathcal {s} _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}}, \boldsymbol {v}} \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\| \left\| \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \boldsymbol {g} + a \theta z + \mathbf {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \boldsymbol {v} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \\ + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} h\right) ^ {t} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \theta_ {\perp} + \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {- 1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \left(\mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp}\right) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp} + \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta - \widetilde {\ell} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {w}). \tag {b.49} \\ \end{array}
$$

here we used the assumption that  $\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$  is an eigenvector of  $\pmb{\sigma}$  which in turn implies that

$$
p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {- 1 / 2} p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \left(p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp}\right) = p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {- 1 / 2} p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} = p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {- 1 / 2} \left(p _ {\mu} + p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp}\right) \sigma^ {1 / 2} p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} = p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp}. \tag {b.50}
$$

we next optimize over  $\pmb{\theta}$  using lemma below and its proof is deferred to appendix e.4.

lemma b.1 for a given vector  $\pmb{r}$  and  $\alpha \geq 0$  consider the following optimization

$$
\min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {p}} \left\langle \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {r}, \left(\mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp}\right) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp} \right\rangle \tag {b.51}
$$

$$
\text {s u b j e c t} \left. \right.\left\|\left(\mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp}\right) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} = \alpha \tag {b.52}
$$

under the assumption that  $\mathsf{p}_{\pmb{\mu}}\pmb{\sigma}^{1/2}\mathsf{p}_{\pmb{\mu}}^{\perp} = 0$ , the optimal value of this optimization is given by  $-\alpha \left\| \mathsf{p}_{\pmb{\mu}}^{\perp}\pmb{r} \right\|_{\ell_2}$ .

now note that

$$
\left| \boldsymbol {\theta} \right| = \left| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right| \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}},
$$

$$
\alpha = \left\| \left(\mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp}\right) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} = \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \leq c _ {\max} ^ {1 / 2} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}
$$

where in the second line we used assumption 1(b), (d). since  $\pmb{\theta} \in s_{\pmb{\theta}}$  a bounded set, we can choose  $k' > 0$  large enough so that  $0 \leq |\theta|, \alpha \leq k'$  and hence so do the optimization over this bounded range. that said, we use lemma b.1 with  $\boldsymbol{r} = \frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}} \boldsymbol{h} + \sigma^{-1/2} \mathsf{p}_{\mu}^{\perp} \boldsymbol{w}$ , to simplify the ao problem as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {0 \leq \beta \leq k, \boldsymbol {w}} \min  _ {0 \leq \alpha , | \theta | \leq k ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {v}} \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\| \alpha \boldsymbol {g} + a \theta \boldsymbol {z} + \mathbf {1} \left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \boldsymbol {v} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \\ - \alpha \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {- 1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} + \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta - \widetilde {\ell} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {w}) \tag {b.53} \\ \end{array}
$$

to continue we shall calculate the conjugate function  $\widetilde{\ell}$ . this is the subject of the next lemma and we refer to appendix e.5 for its proof.

lemma b.2 the conjugate of the function

$$
\ell (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {\theta}) = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(v _ {i} - \varepsilon \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {q}}\right)
$$

with respect to  $\pmb{\theta}$  is equal to

$$
\widetilde {\ell} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {w}) = \sup  _ {\gamma \geq 0} \quad \gamma \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} - \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(v _ {i} - \varepsilon \gamma\right).
$$

using the above lemma we have

$$
-\widetilde{\ell} (\boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {w}) = -\left(\sup_{\gamma \geq 0}\quad \gamma   \| \boldsymbol {w}\|_{\ell_{p}} - \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\ell \left(v_{i} - \varepsilon \gamma\right)\right) = \inf_{\gamma \geq 0}\quad -\gamma   \| \boldsymbol {w}\|_{\ell_{p}} + \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\ell \left(v_{i} - \varepsilon \gamma\right).
$$

plugging this into (b.53) we arrive at

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {0 \leq \beta \leq k, \boldsymbol {w}} \min  _ {0 \leq \alpha , | \theta | \leq k ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {v}, 0 \leq \gamma} \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\| \alpha \boldsymbol {g} + a \theta \boldsymbol {z} + \mathbf {1} \left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \boldsymbol {v} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \\ - \alpha \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} + \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta - \gamma \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell (v _ {i} - \varepsilon \gamma) \tag {b.54} \\ \end{array}
$$

note that when  $p \geq 1$  the objective is jointly concave in  $(\pmb{w},\beta)$  and jointly convex in  $\alpha, \theta, \pmb{v}$  and therefore we can switch the orders of min and max.

we next focus on optimization over  $\pmb{v}$ . using the observation that for all  $x \in \mathbb{r}$ ,  $\min_{\tau \geq 0} \frac{\tau}{2} + \frac{x^2}{2\tau} = x$  we write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v}} \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\| \alpha \boldsymbol {g} + a \theta \boldsymbol {z} + \mathbf {1} \left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \boldsymbol {v} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell (v _ {i} - \varepsilon \gamma) \\ = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v}} \inf  _ {\tau_ {g} \geq 0} \frac {\beta}{2 \tau_ {g} n} \left\| \alpha \boldsymbol {g} + a \theta \boldsymbol {z} + \mathbf {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \boldsymbol {v} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell (v _ {i} - \varepsilon \gamma) \\ = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v}} \inf  _ {\tau_ {g} \geq 0} \frac {\beta}{2 \tau_ {g} n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\alpha g _ {i} + a \theta z _ {i} + \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - v _ {i}\right) ^ {2} + \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell (v _ {i} - \varepsilon \gamma) \\ = \min  _ {\widetilde {v} _ {i}} \inf  _ {\tau_ {g} \geq 0} \frac {\beta}{2 \tau_ {g} n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\alpha g _ {i} + a \theta z _ {i} + \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \widetilde {v} _ {i} - \varepsilon \gamma\right) ^ {2} + \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell (\widetilde {v} _ {i}) \tag {b.55} \\ \end{array}
$$

as a result (b.54) can be rewritten as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max_{0\leq \beta \leq k,\boldsymbol {w}}\min_{0\leq \alpha ,|\theta |\leq k^{\prime},\widetilde{v},0\leq \gamma}\inf_{\tau_{g}\geq 0}\frac{\beta}{2\tau_{g}n}\left\| \alpha \boldsymbol {g} + a\theta \boldsymbol {z} + \mathbf{1}\left\| \boldsymbol {\mu}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\theta -\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}} -\varepsilon \gamma \mathbf{1}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2} + \frac{\beta\tau_{g}}{2} \\ - \alpha \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} + \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta - \gamma \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell (\widetilde {v} _ {i}) \tag {b.56} \\ \end{array}
$$

we note that since the quadratic over linear function is jointly convex the above loss is jointly convex in the parameters  $(\alpha, \gamma, \theta, \tau_g, \widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}})$ . also for  $p \geq 1$  the  $\| \cdot \|_{\ell_p}$  is convex and thus the objective is also jointly concave in  $(\beta, \boldsymbol{w})$ .

we recall the definition of the moreau envelope of function  $\ell$  at a point  $x$  with parameter  $\mu$ , that is given by

$$
e _ {\ell} (x; \mu) := \min  _ {t} \frac {1}{2 \mu} (x - t) ^ {2} + \ell (t). \tag {b.57}
$$

we can now rewrite equation (b.56) in terms of moreau envelope of the loss function  $\ell$ .

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {\widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}}} \inf  _ {\tau_ {g} \geq 0} \frac {\beta}{2 \tau_ {g} n} \left\| \alpha \boldsymbol {g} + a \theta \boldsymbol {z} + \mathbf {1} \left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} - \varepsilon \gamma \mathbf {1} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {i}) \\ = \inf  _ {\tau_ {g} \geq 0} \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} e _ {\ell} \left(\alpha g _ {i} + a \theta z _ {i} + \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \varepsilon \gamma ; \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) \tag {b.58} \\ \end{array}
$$

thus (b.56) can be rewritten in the form

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {0 \leq \beta \leq k, \boldsymbol {w}} \min  _ {0 \leq \alpha , | \theta | \leq k ^ {\prime}, 0 \leq \gamma} \inf  _ {\tau_ {g} \geq 0} \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} e _ {\ell} \left(\alpha g _ {i} + a \theta z _ {i} + \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \varepsilon \gamma ; \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) \\ - \alpha \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} + \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta - \gamma \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} \tag {b.59} \\ \end{array}
$$

note that since (b.56) is jointly convex in  $(\alpha, \gamma, \theta, \tau_g, \widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}})$  and jointly concave in  $(\beta, \boldsymbol{w})$  and partial minimization preserves convexity thus (b.59) is jointly convex in  $(\alpha, \gamma, \theta, \tau_g)$  and jointly concave in  $(\beta, \boldsymbol{w})$ .

# b.3.2 scalarization of the auxiliary optimization problem

the auxiliary problem (b.59) is in terms of high-dimensional vectors  $\pmb{g}, \pmb{z}, \pmb{h}, \pmb{w}, \pmb{\mu}$ . we turn this problem into a scalar optimization by taking the pointwise limit of its objective and then showing that such convergence indeed holds in a uniform sense and therefore the minimax value also converges to that of the limit objective.

note that by definition of the moreau envelope, for all  $x$  and  $\mu$  we have

$$
e _ {\ell} (x; \mu) \leq \frac {1}{2 \mu} (x - x) ^ {2} + \ell (x) = \ell (x) = \log (1 + e ^ {- x}) \leq \log 2 + | x |.
$$

hence,

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ e _ {\ell} \left(\alpha g + a \theta z + \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \varepsilon \gamma ; \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) \right] \leq \log 2 + \mathbb {e} \left[ | \alpha g + a \theta z + \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \varepsilon \gamma | \right] <   \infty ,
$$

for any finite value of  $\alpha$ ,  $\theta$  and  $\gamma$ . therefore by an application of the weak law of large numbers, we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} e _ {\ell} \left(\alpha g _ {i} + a \theta z _ {i} + \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \varepsilon \gamma ; \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right)\rightarrow \mathbb {e} \left[ e _ {\ell} \left(\alpha g + a \theta z + \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \varepsilon \gamma ; \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right)\right] \\ = \mathbb {e} \left[ e _ {\ell} \left(\sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + a ^ {2} \theta^ {2}} g + \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \theta - \varepsilon \gamma ; \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

we define the expected moreau envelope  $l(a, b, \mu) = \mathbb{e}[e_{\ell}(ag + b; \mu)]$ , where the expectation is taken with respect to independent standard normal variable  $g$ .

this simplifies the ao problem as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {0 \leq \beta \leq k, \boldsymbol {w}} \min  _ {0 \leq \alpha , | \theta | \leq k ^ {\prime}, 0 \leq \gamma , \tau_ {g}} \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + l \left(\sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + a ^ {2} \theta^ {2}}, \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}   \theta - \varepsilon \gamma , \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) \\ - \alpha \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} + \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta - \gamma \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}. \tag {b.60} \\ \end{array}
$$

we note that since (b.59) is jointly convex in  $(\alpha, \gamma, \theta, \tau_g)$  and jointly concave in  $(\beta, \boldsymbol{w})$  and expectation preserves convexity thus the objective in (b.60) is also jointly convex in  $(\alpha, \gamma, \theta, \tau_g)$  and jointly concave in  $(\beta, \boldsymbol{w})$  and by sinov's theorem we can flip the maximization over  $\boldsymbol{w}$  and the minimization to arrive at

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {0 \leq \beta \leq k} \min  _ {0 \leq \alpha , | \theta | \leq k ^ {\prime}, 0 <   \gamma , \tau_ {g}} \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + l \left(\sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + a ^ {2} \theta^ {2}}, \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}   \theta - \varepsilon \gamma , \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) \\ \left. - \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left\{\alpha \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta + \gamma \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} \right\}. \right. \tag {b.61} \\ \end{array}
$$

by our asymptotic setting (cf. definition 1, part (c)),  $\varepsilon = \varepsilon_0\| \pmb {\mu}\|_{\ell_p}$  for a constant  $\varepsilon_0$ . we let  $\gamma_0\coloneqq \gamma \| \pmb {\mu}\|_{\ell_p}$  and rewriting (b.61) in terms of  $\gamma_0$  in lieu of  $\gamma$  we arrive at

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max_{0\leq \beta \leq k}\min_{0\leq \alpha ,|\theta |\leq k^{\prime},0 <   \gamma_{0} <   k^{\prime \prime},0 <   \tau_{g}}\frac{\beta\tau_{g}}{2} +l\left(\sqrt{\alpha^{2} + a^{2}\theta^{2}},\| \boldsymbol {\mu}\|_{\ell_{2}}  \theta -\varepsilon_{0}\gamma_{0},\frac{\tau_{g}}{\beta}\right) \\ - \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left\{\alpha \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta + \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} \right\}. \tag {b.62} \\ \end{array}
$$

- optimization over  $\mathbf{w}$ . continuing with optimization over  $\mathbf{w}$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \alpha \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {- 1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta + \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} \\ = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \tau_ {h} \geq 0} \frac {\alpha}{2 \tau_ {h}} \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta + \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} \\ = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \tau_ {h} \geq 0} \frac {\alpha}{2 \tau_ {h}} \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta + \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}, \tag {b.63} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step we used that  $\mathsf{p}_{\pmb{\mu}}^{\perp}\pmb{\sigma}^{-1/2}\mathsf{p}_{\pmb{\mu}} = 0$ , which follows from assumption 5. note that the above loss is jointly convex in  $(\pmb{w}, \tau_h)$ . so that continuing from (b.61) the overall objective is jointly convex in  $(\alpha, \gamma, \theta, \tau_g)$  and jointly concave in  $(\beta, \pmb{w}, \tau_h)$ .

let  $\tilde{\pmb{w}}\coloneqq \pmb{\sigma}^{-1 / 2}\pmb{w}$ . the optimization over  $\pmb{w}$  can be written as

$$
\min  _ {\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}} \frac {1}{2} \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + f (\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}), \tag {b.64}
$$

where

$$
f (\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}) := - \langle \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \rangle \frac {\theta \tau_ {h}}{\alpha} + \frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {p}}.
$$

let  $\tilde{w}^*$  be the optimal solution. then,

$$
- \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} + \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*}\right) \in \partial f \left(\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*}\right). \tag {b.65}
$$

by the conjugate subgradient theorem, this implies that

$$
\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*} \in \partial f ^ {*} \left(- \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} + \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*}\right)\right).
$$

let  $\pmb{t}^{*} \coloneqq \frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}} \pmb{h} + \tilde{\pmb{w}}^{*}$ , then writing the above equation in terms of  $t$ ,

$$
\boldsymbol {t} ^ {*} - \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} \in \partial f ^ {*} \left(- p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {t} ^ {*}\right). \tag {b.66}
$$

therefore,

$$
- \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \left(\boldsymbol {t} ^ {*} - \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right) \in - \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \partial f ^ {*} \left(- \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {t} ^ {*}\right). \tag {b.67}
$$

equation (b.67) is equivalent to saying that

$$
\boldsymbol {t} ^ {*} \in \arg \min  _ {\boldsymbol {t}} \frac {1}{2} \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} - \boldsymbol {t}\right) \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + f ^ {*} (- \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {t}). \tag {b.68}
$$

lemma b.3 for function  $f: \mathbb{r}^p \mapsto \mathbb{r}$  given by

$$
f (\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}) := - \langle \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \rangle \frac {\theta \tau_ {h}}{\alpha} + \frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {p}},
$$

its convex conjugate reads as

$$
f ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {u}) = \mathbb {1} _ {s} (\boldsymbol {u}), \quad s := \left\{\boldsymbol {u}: \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {u} + \frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {q}} \leq \frac {\gamma \tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \right\}, \quad \mathbb {1} _ {s} (\boldsymbol {u}) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} 0 & \text {i f} \boldsymbol {u} \in s \\ \infty & \text {i f} \boldsymbol {u} \notin s \end{array} \right.
$$

the proof of lemma b.3 is delegated to appendix e.6.

define  $\mathcal{b} \coloneqq \{\pmb{\mu}\}^{\perp} \cap -\mathcal{s}$ . then (b.68) implies that

$$
\mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {t} ^ {*} = \mathsf {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right)\right). \tag {b.69}
$$

lemma b.4 for a convex set  $s$  and  $\mathcal{b} \coloneqq \{\pmb{\mu}\}^{\perp} \cap s$ , we have  $\mathsf{p}_{\mathcal{b}} \mathsf{p}_{\pmb{\mu}}^{\perp} = \mathsf{p}_{\mathcal{b}}$ .

we refer to appendix e.7 for the proof of lemma b.4.

using lemma b.4 and (b.69) we obtain

$$
\mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {t} ^ {*} = \mathsf {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right).
$$

recalling definition of  $\pmb{t}^*$  this implies

$$
\mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*} = \mathrm {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right) - \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h}. \tag {b.70}
$$

now note that for  $p > 1$ ,  $\nabla \| \boldsymbol{w}\|_{\ell_p} = \frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{\ell_p}^{p - 1}} [|w_1|^{p - 1}\mathrm{sign}(w_1),\ldots ,|w_p|^{p - 1}\mathrm{sign}(w_p)]^\top$ . therefore in this case  $\langle \nabla \| \boldsymbol{w}\|_{\ell_p},\boldsymbol{w}\rangle = \| \boldsymbol{w}\|_{\ell_p}$ . similarly, for  $p = 1$  for any  $\boldsymbol{s} \in \partial \| \boldsymbol{w}\|_{\ell_p}$  we have  $\langle \boldsymbol{s},\boldsymbol{w}\rangle = \| \boldsymbol{w}\|_{\ell_p}$ . therefore, for all  $p \geq 1$  for any  $\boldsymbol{s} \in \partial \| \boldsymbol{w}\|_{\ell_p}$  we have  $\langle \boldsymbol{s},\boldsymbol{w}\rangle = \| \boldsymbol{w}\|_{\ell_p}$ .

therefore, for the defined function  $f$  and any  $\boldsymbol{s} \in \partial f(\tilde{\boldsymbol{w}})$  there is a vector  $\left. \widetilde{\boldsymbol{s}} \in \partial \| \boldsymbol{x} \|_{\ell_p} \right|_{\boldsymbol{x} = \boldsymbol{\sigma}^{1/2} \tilde{\boldsymbol{w}}} \text{ such that}$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\langle \boldsymbol {s}, \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\rangle = \left\langle - \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \frac {\theta \tau_ {h}}{\alpha} + \frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {s}}, \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\rangle \\ = - \left\langle \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\rangle \frac {\theta \tau_ {h}}{\alpha} + \frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \left\langle \widetilde {\boldsymbol {s}}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\rangle \\ = - \left\langle \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\rangle \frac {\theta \tau_ {h}}{\alpha} + \frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {p}} \\ = f (\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}). \tag {b.71} \\ \end{array}
$$

therefore by invoking (b.65)

$$
\begin{array}{l} f \left(\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*}\right) = \left\langle - p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} + \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*}\right), \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*} \right\rangle \\ = \left\langle - \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*}, p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*} \right\rangle \\ = \left\langle - p _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right), p _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right) - \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} p _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} \right\rangle \\ = - \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right) \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\langle \mathsf {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right), \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} \right\rangle . \tag {b.72} \\ \end{array}
$$

putting things together, the optimal value of objective (b.64) over  $\pmb{w}$  is given by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min _ {\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}} \frac {1}{2} \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + f (\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}) \\ = \frac {1}{2} \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + f (\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {*}) \\ = \frac {1}{2} \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right) \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} - \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right) \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\langle \mathsf {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right), \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} \right\rangle \\ = - \frac {1}{2} \left\| \mathrm {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right) \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \left\langle \mathrm {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right), \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} \right\rangle \\ = \frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 n} \left\| p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \left\| p _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right) - \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \\ = \frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 n} \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right) - \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 n} \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \boldsymbol {h} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \\ = \frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \left\| \mathrm {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h}\right) - \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}. \tag {b.73} \\ \end{array}
$$

following the argument after (b.63) since the objective is jointly convex in  $(\alpha, \gamma, \theta, \tau_g)$  and jointly concave in  $(\beta, \boldsymbol{w}, \tau_h)$  and partial maximization preserves concavity after plugging the above the objective is jointly convex in  $(\alpha, \gamma, \theta, \tau_g)$  and jointly concave in  $(\beta, \tau_h)$ .

- on projection  $\mathsf{p}_{\mathcal{b}}$ . as part of our scalarization process of the auxiliary optimization problem, in the next lemma we provide an alternative characterization of the distance  $\| \mathsf{p}_{\mathcal{b}}(\boldsymbol{h}) - \boldsymbol{h} \|_{\ell_2}$ , and refer to appendix e.8 for its proof.

lemma b.5 recall the set  $\mathcal{b} \coloneqq \{\pmb{\mu}\}^{\perp} \cap -\mathcal{s}$ , where  $\mathcal{s}$  is given by

$$
s := \left\{\boldsymbol {u}: \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {u} + \frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {q}} \leq \frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \right\}.
$$

also, suppose that  $\pmb{\sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}} = a\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$ . then, for any vector  $\pmb{h}$  the following holds:

$$
\frac {1}{2} \left\| \mathsf {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} (\boldsymbol {h}) - \boldsymbol {h} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} = \sup  _ {\lambda \geq 0, \nu} e _ {q, \boldsymbol {\sigma}} \left(\boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {h} - \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \lambda\right) - \lambda \left(\frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {q} + \nu \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} \quad (b. 7 4)
$$

using equation (b.73) along with lemma b.5 we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min _ {\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}} \frac {1}{2} \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + f (\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}) \\ = \inf  _ {\lambda \geq 0, \nu} \frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda \left(\frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}}\right) ^ {q} - \frac {\nu \beta}{\sqrt {n}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} + \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} - e _ {q, \boldsymbol {\sigma}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {h} - \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \lambda\right) \tag {b.75} \\ \end{array}
$$

recalling equation (b.63) we have

$$
\min _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \alpha \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta + \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \min _ {\tau_ {h}, \lambda \geq 0, \nu} \frac {\alpha}{\tau_ {h}} \left\{\frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda \left(\frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}}\right) ^ {q} - \frac {\nu \beta}{\sqrt {n}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} + \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} - e _ {q, \sigma} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {h} - \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \lambda\right) \right\} \\ + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \min  _ {\tau_ {h}, \lambda \geq 0, \nu} \frac {\alpha}{\tau_ {h}} \left\{\frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda_ {0} \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \gamma_ {0}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {q} - \frac {\nu \beta}{\sqrt {n}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} + \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} - e _ {q, \boldsymbol {\sigma}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {h} - \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}\right) \right\} \\ + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2} \tag {b.76} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used the reparameterization  $\lambda_0 \coloneqq \frac{\lambda}{\|\pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_p}^q}$ . next we use assumption 7 to take the limit of the above expression as  $n \to \infty$ . by definition of function  $\mathsf{e}$  we have

$$
\lim _ {n \to \infty} e _ {q, \pmb {\sigma}} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \pmb {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \pmb {h} - \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right) \widetilde {\pmb {\mu}}; \lambda_ {0} \| \pmb {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}\right) = \mathsf {e} \left(\beta , \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right); \lambda_ {0}\right).
$$

also, since  $\pmb{h} \sim \mathsf{n}(0, \pmb{i}_d)$  we have

$$
\lim _ {n \to \infty} \frac {1}{n} \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{\delta}, \quad \lim _ {n \to \infty} \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} = 0.
$$

using the above two equations in (b.76) we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left\{\alpha \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} + \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} - \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \theta + \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} \right\} \\ = \frac {\alpha}{\tau_ {h}} \left\{\frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 \delta} + \lambda_ {0} \left(\frac {\gamma_ {0} \tau_ {h}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {q} + \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} - \mathsf {e} \left(\beta , \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right); \lambda_ {0}\right) \right\} + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2}. \tag {b.77} \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, incorporating the above equation in (b.62) and using assumption 2, the ao problem simplifies to:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max_{0\leq \beta \leq k}\min_{0\leq \alpha ,|\theta |\leq k^{\prime},0 <   \gamma_{0} <   k^{\prime \prime},0 <   \tau_{g}}\frac{\beta\tau_{g}}{2} +l\left(\sqrt{\alpha^{2} + a^{2}\theta^{2}},v\theta -\varepsilon_{0}\gamma_{0},\frac{\tau_{g}}{\beta}\right) \\ - \min  _ {\tau_ {h}, \lambda_ {0} \geq 0, \nu} \left[ \frac {\alpha}{\tau_ {h}} \left\{\frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 \delta} + \lambda_ {0} \left(\frac {\gamma_ {0} \tau_ {h}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {q} + \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} - \mathsf {e} \left(\beta , \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right); \lambda_ {0}\right) \right\} + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2} \right] \tag {b.78} \\ \end{array}
$$

now recall the argument after (b.73) that the objective is jointly convex in  $(\alpha, \gamma, \theta, \tau_g)$  and jointly concave in  $(\beta, \tau_h)$ . we used lemma b.5 to provide alternative characterization for quantity  $\| \mathsf{p}_{\mathcal{b}}(\pmb{h}) - \pmb{h} \|_{\ell_2}^2$ , which led into introducing the new variables  $\lambda_0, \nu$ . therefore, the objective (b.78), after maximization over  $\lambda_0, \nu$ , is jointly convex in  $(\alpha, \gamma_0, \theta, \tau_g)$  and jointly concave in  $(\beta, \tau_h)$ . because of that we can interchange the order of minimization and minimization over using sion's minimax theorem to get the following.

$$
\min  _ {\theta , 0 \leq \alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \tau_ {g}} \max  _ {0 \leq \beta , \tau_ {h}} d _ {\mathrm {n s}} (\alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \theta , \tau_ {g}, \beta , \tau_ {h})
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} d _ {\mathrm {n s}} (\alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \theta , \tau_ {g}, \beta , \tau_ {h}) = \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + l \left(\sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + a ^ {2} \theta^ {2}}, v \theta - \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0}, \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) \\ - \min  _ {\lambda_ {0} \geq 0, \nu} \left[ \frac {\alpha}{\tau_ {h}} \left\{\frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 \delta} + \lambda_ {0} \left(\frac {\gamma_ {0} \tau_ {h}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {q} + \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} - e \left(\beta , \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right); \lambda_ {0}\right) \right\} + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2} \right]. \tag {b.79} \\ \end{array}
$$

# b.3.3 uniform convergence of the auxiliary problem to its scalarized version

we showed that the auxiliary optimization objective converges pointwise to the function  $d_{\mathrm{ns}}$  given by (b.79). however, we are interested in the minimax optimal solution of the auxiliary problem and need to have convergence of optimal points to the minimax solution of  $d$ . what is required for this aim is (local) uniform convergence of the auxiliary objective to function  $d$ . this can be shown by following similar arguments as in [tah18, lemma a.5] that is essentially based on a result known as "convexity lemma" in the literature (see e.g. [lm08, lemma 7.75]) by which pointwise convergence of convex functions implies uniform convergence in compact subsets.

# b.3.4 uniqueness of the solution of the ao problem

first note that since the loss  $\ell(t)$  is a convex function and  $\frac{1}{2\mu}(x - t)^2$  is jointly convex in  $(x, t, \mu)$ , then  $\frac{1}{2\mu}(x - t)^2 + \ell(t)$  is jointly convex in  $(x, t, \mu)$ . given that partial minimization preserves convexity, the moreau envelope  $e_{\ell}(x; \mu)$  is jointly convex in  $(x, \mu)$ . in addition, by using the result of [tah18, lemma 4.4] the expected moreau envelope of a convex function is jointly "strictly" convex (indeed this holds without requiring any strong or strict convexity assumption on the function itself). an application of this result to our case implies that  $l(a, b, \mu)$  is jointly strictly convex in  $\mathbb{r}_{\geq 0} \times \mathbb{r} \times \mathbb{r}_{\geq 0}$ .

in addition, as we argued before the function  $d_{\mathrm{ns}}$  given by (b.79) is jointly convex in  $(\alpha, \gamma_0, \theta, \tau_g)$  and jointly concave in  $(\beta, \tau_h)$ . hence, using strict convexity of  $l(a, b, \mu)$ , the function  $d_{\mathrm{ns}}$  is indeed jointly "strictly" convex in  $(\alpha, \gamma_0, \theta, \tau_g)$  and jointly concave in  $(\beta, \tau_h)$ .

as the next step, we note that  $\max_{\beta,\tau_h} d(\alpha,\gamma_0,\theta,\tau_g,\beta,\tau_h)$  is strictly convex in  $(\alpha,\gamma_0,\theta,\tau_g)$ . this follows from the fact that if a function  $f(\pmb{x},\pmb{y})$  is strictly convex in  $\pmb{x}$ , then  $\max_{\pmb{y}} f(\pmb{x},\pmb{y})$

is also strictly convex in  $\pmb{x}$ . moreover, by using the result of [tah18, lemma c.5] we have that  $\inf_{\tau_g > 0}\max_{\beta ,\tau_h}d(\alpha ,\gamma_0,\theta ,\tau_g,\beta ,\tau_h)$  is strictly convex in  $(\alpha ,\gamma_0,\theta)$  and therefore has a unique minimizer  $(\alpha_{*},\gamma_{0*},\theta_{*})$ . this concludes the part (a) of the theorem and the given scalar minimax optimization to characterize the limiting behavior of parameter of interest  $\alpha ,\gamma_0,\theta$

part (b) of the theorem follows readily from our definition of parameters  $\alpha$ ,  $\theta$  and  $\gamma$ . part (c) of the theorem also follows from combining lemma 2.1 with part (b) of the theorem.

this completes the proof of theorem 6.4.

# b.4 proof of remark 6.1

we start by establishing an explicit expression for the weighted moreau envelope  $e_{q,\sigma}$  for case of  $p = q = 2$ .

lemma b.6 we have

$$
e _ {2, \boldsymbol {\sigma}} (\boldsymbol {x}; \lambda) = \lambda \left\| (\boldsymbol {\sigma} + 2 \lambda \boldsymbol {i}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}
$$

the proof of lemma b.6 is given in appendix e.9.

suppose that items  $(i),(ii)$  in the statement of the remark are satisfied. we then prove that assumption 6 and 7 hold.

proof [verification of assumption 6] to check assumption 6 for  $p = q = 2$ , we use lemma b.6 to get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} e _ {2, \boldsymbol {i} + b _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma}} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {i} + b _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) ^ {- 1} \left\{\frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\}; b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}\right) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \left\|\left(\left(1 + 2 b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}\right) \boldsymbol {i} + b _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {i} + b _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}\right)\right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \tag {b.80} \\ \end{array}
$$

consider a singular value decomposition  $\pmb{\sigma} = \pmb{u}\pmb{s}\pmb{u}^t$  with  $\pmb{s} = \mathrm{diag}(s_1,\dots,s_d)$ , and the first column of  $\pmb{u}$  being  $\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$  and  $s_1 = a^2$  (recall that  $\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$  is a singular value of  $\pmb{\sigma}$  with eigenvalue  $a^2$ .) also let  $\tilde{\pmb{h}} := \pmb{u}^t\pmb{h} \sim \mathsf{n}(0,\pmb{i}_d)$ . continuing from (b.80) we write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} e _ {2, \boldsymbol {i} + b _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma}} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {i} + b _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) ^ {- 1} \left\{\frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\}; b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}\right) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{n} b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {u} \left(\left(1 + 2 b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}\right) \boldsymbol {i} + b _ {0} \boldsymbol {s}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {i} + b _ {0} \boldsymbol {s}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {u} ^ {t} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{2} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \sqrt {n} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}\right)\right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \tag {b.81} \\ \end{array}
$$

write  $\mathbf{u} = [\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mu}},\tilde{\mathbf{u}} ]$  and  $\tilde{\pmb{s}} = \mathrm{diag}(s_2,\dots ,s_d)$ . in addition, define  $\tilde{\pmb{h}} \coloneqq \tilde{\pmb{u}}^t\pmb {h}\sim \mathsf{n}(0,\pmb{i}_{d - 1})$ . we then have

$$
\boldsymbol {u} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} = \left( \begin{array}{c} 0 \\ \boldsymbol {s} ^ {1 / 2} \tilde {\boldsymbol {h}} \end{array} \right), \quad \boldsymbol {u} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} = \boldsymbol {e} _ {1}, \quad \lim  _ {n \to \infty} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} = \sigma_ {m, 2},
$$

in probability, with the last limit following from item (i) in the statement remark 6.1. using the

above identities in (b.81) we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} e _ {2, i + b _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma}} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {i} + b _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) ^ {- 1} \left\{\frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\}; b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}\right) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{n} \left\{\frac {c _ {1} ^ {2} n}{4} \cdot \frac {1}{(1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2} + b _ {0} a ^ {2}) (1 + b _ {0} a ^ {2})} + \frac {c _ {0} ^ {2}}{4} \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {d} \frac {s _ {i} \tilde {h} _ {i} ^ {2}}{(1 + b _ {0} s _ {i}) (1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2} + b _ {0} s _ {i})} \right\} \\ = b _ {1} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2} \left\{\frac {c _ {1} ^ {2}}{4 (1 + b _ {0} a ^ {2}) (1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2} + b _ {0} a ^ {2})} + \frac {c _ {0} ^ {2}}{4 \delta} \lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{d} \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {d} \frac {s _ {i} \tilde {h} _ {i} ^ {2}}{(1 + b _ {0} s _ {i}) (1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2} + b _ {0} s _ {i})} \right\} \tag {b.82} \\ \end{array}
$$

define  $\nu_{i} := s_{i}(1 + b_{0}s_{i})^{-1}(1 + 2b_{1}\sigma_{m,2}^{2} + b_{0}s_{i})^{-1}$ . then the last sum reads as  $\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=2}^{d}\nu_{i}\tilde{h}_{i}^{2}$ . recall that  $\tilde{\pmb{h}} \sim \mathsf{n}(0,\pmb{i}_{d-1})$ . therefore, by applying the kolmogorov's criterion of slln the above limit exists (almost surely and so in probability as well) provided that  $\frac{1}{d^2}\sum_{i=2}^{d}\nu_{i}^{2}\mathrm{var}(\tilde{h}_{i}^{2}) < \infty$ . we note that  $var(\tilde{h}_{i}^{2}) = 2$  and since  $\nu_{i} \geq 0$ , we have

$$
\frac {1}{d ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {d} \nu_ {i} ^ {2} \leq \left(\frac {1}{d} \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {d} \nu_ {i}\right) ^ {2}.
$$

hence it suffices to show that  $\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=2}^{d}\nu_i < \infty$ . now by item (ii) of remark 6.1, the empirical distribution of eigenvalues of  $\pmb{\sigma}$  converges weakly to a distribution  $\rho$  with stieltjes transform  $s_{\rho}(z) \coloneqq \int \frac{\rho(t)}{z - t} \mathrm{d}t$ . we write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {s _ {i}}{\left(1 + b _ {0} s _ {i}\right) \left(1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2} + b _ {0} s _ {i}\right)} \\ = \frac {1}{2 b _ {0} b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \left\{- \frac {1}{1 + b _ {0} s _ {i}} + \frac {1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2} + b _ {0} s _ {i}} \right\} \\ = \frac {1}{2 b _ {0} ^ {2} b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \left\{- \frac {1}{\frac {1}{b _ {0}} + s _ {i}} + \frac {1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{\frac {1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{b _ {0}} + s _ {i}} \right\} \tag {b.83} \\ \end{array}
$$

therefore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{d} \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {d} \nu_ {i} = \lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{d} \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {d} \frac {s _ {i}}{\left(1 + b _ {0} s _ {i}\right)\left(1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {\bar {m} , 2} ^ {2} + b _ {0} s _ {i}\right)} \\ = \frac {1}{2 b _ {0} ^ {2} b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{d} \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {d} \left\{- \frac {1}{\frac {1}{b _ {0}} + s _ {i}} + \frac {1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{\frac {1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{b _ {0}} + s _ {i}} \right\} \\ = \frac {1}{2 b _ {0} ^ {2} b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \left\{s _ {\rho} \left(- \frac {1}{b _ {0}}\right) - \left(1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2}\right) s _ {\rho} \left(- \frac {1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{b _ {0}}\right) \right\}. \tag {b.84} \\ \end{array}
$$

it is worth noting that although the sum is over  $2 \leq i \leq d$ , the term for  $i = 1$  is  $o(1 / d)$  and is negligible in the limit. therefore, we can include that in our calculation above. by using equation (b.84)

in (b.82) we get that assumption 6 holds with

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathsf {f} (c _ {0}, c _ {1}; b _ {0}, b _ {1}) = \frac {b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2} c _ {1} ^ {2}}{4 (1 + b _ {0} a ^ {2}) (1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2} + b _ {0} a ^ {2})} \\ + \frac {b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2} c _ {0} ^ {2}}{8 \delta b _ {0} ^ {2} b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \left\{s _ {\rho} \left(- \frac {1}{b _ {0}}\right) - \left(1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2}\right) s _ {\rho} \left(- \frac {1 + 2 b _ {1} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{b _ {0}}\right) \right\}. \tag {b.85} \\ \end{array}
$$

proof [verification of assumption 7] to check assumption 7 we use lemma b.6 and write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} e _ {2, \boldsymbol {\sigma}} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {h} - c _ {1} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}\right) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \left\| (\boldsymbol {\sigma} + 2 \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {i}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {h} - c _ {1} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}\right)\right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \left\|\left(\boldsymbol {\sigma} + 2 \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {i}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} - c _ {1} a \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}\right)\right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \tag {b.86} \\ \end{array}
$$

consider a singular value decomposition  $\pmb{\sigma} = \pmb{u}\pmb{s}\pmb{u}^t$  with  $\pmb{s} = \mathrm{diag}(s_1,\dots,s_d)$ , and the first column of  $\pmb{u}$  being  $\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$  and  $s_1 = a^2$  (recall that  $\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$  is a singular value of  $\pmb{\sigma}$  with eigenvalue  $a^2$ .) also let  $\tilde{\pmb{h}} := \pmb{u}^t\pmb{h} \sim \mathsf{n}(0,\pmb{i}_d)$ . continuing from (b.86) we write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} e _ {2, \boldsymbol {\sigma}} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {h} - c _ {1} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}\right) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{n} \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {u} (\boldsymbol {s} + 2 \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {i}) ^ {- 1 / 2} (c _ {0} \tilde {\boldsymbol {h}} - c _ {1} \sqrt {n} a \boldsymbol {e} _ {1}) \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {\lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{n} \left\{\frac {(c _ {0} \tilde {h} _ {1} - c _ {1} \sqrt {n} a) ^ {2}}{a ^ {2} + 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} + \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {d} \frac {c _ {0} ^ {2} \tilde {h} _ {i} ^ {2}}{s _ {i} + 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \right\} \\ = \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2} \left\{\frac {c _ {1} ^ {2} a ^ {2}}{a ^ {2} + 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} + \frac {1}{\delta} \lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{d} \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {d} \frac {c _ {0} ^ {2} \tilde {h} _ {i} ^ {2}}{s _ {i} + 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \right\}. \tag {b.87} \\ \end{array}
$$

by applying the kolmogorov's criterion of slln the above limit exists (almost surely and so in probability as well) provided that  $\frac{1}{d^2}\sum_{i=2}^{d}\frac{1}{(s_i + 2\lambda_0\sigma_{m,2}^2)^2} < \infty$ . note that since  $\lambda_0, s_i \geq 0$ , we have

$$
\frac {1}{d ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {d} \frac {1}{(s _ {i} + 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}) ^ {2}} \leq \frac {1}{d ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {d} \frac {1}{4 \lambda_ {0} ^ {2} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {4}} \rightarrow 0.
$$

by using the lln we obtain that the summation in (b.87) converges (almost surely) to its expectation. now recalling item (ii) in remark 6.1, we know that the empirical distribution of eigenvalues of  $\pmb{\sigma}$  converges weakly to a distribution  $\rho$  with stieltjes transform  $s_{\rho}(z)\coloneqq \int \frac{\rho(t)}{z - t}\mathrm{d}t$ , and therefore we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathsf {e} \left(c _ {0}, c _ {1}; \lambda_ {0}\right) := \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} e _ {2, \boldsymbol {\sigma}} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {h} - c _ {1} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}\right) \\ = \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2} \left\{\frac {c _ {1} ^ {2} a ^ {2}}{a ^ {2} + 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} - \frac {c _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta} s _ {\rho} (- 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2}) \right\}. \tag {b.88} \\ \end{array}
$$

# c proofs for isotropic gaussian model (section 4)

this section is devoted to the proof of our theorems for the isotropic gaussian model. we discuss how these theorems can be derived as special cases of our results for the anisotropic model, after some algebraic simplifications.

the claim of theorem 4.1 on the separability threshold is an immediate corollary of theorem 6.1, with  $\pmb{\sigma} = \pmb{i}_{p\times p}$  and  $a = 1$ . we next move to the two other theorems on precise characterization of standard and robust accuracy in the separable and non-separable regimes.

# c.1 proof of theorem 4.3

suppose that assumption 4 in the statement of theorem 4.3 holds. we first show that this assumption implies assumption 6, required by theorem 6.3, in case of  $\pmb{\sigma} = \pmb{i}$  and then show how theorem 4.3 can be derived as a special case of theorem 6.3.

to prove assumption 6(b) for isotropic case, we use the following two properties of the weighted moreau envelop that holds for all  $q \geq 0$ :

$$
e _ {q, \alpha i} (\boldsymbol {x}; \lambda) = \alpha e _ {q, i} \left(\boldsymbol {x}, \frac {\lambda}{\alpha}\right), \tag {c.1}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{b ^ {2}} e _ {q, \boldsymbol {i}} \left(b \boldsymbol {x}; \frac {\lambda}{b ^ {q - 2}}\right) = e _ {q, \boldsymbol {i}} (\boldsymbol {x}; \lambda). \tag {c.2}
$$

combining the above two identities we get

$$
e _ {q, \alpha {\pmb i}} (\alpha^ {- 1} {\pmb x}; \lambda) = \alpha e _ {q, {\pmb i}} \left(\frac {{\pmb x}}{\alpha}; \frac {\lambda}{\alpha}\right) = \frac {\alpha}{b ^ {2}} e _ {q, {\pmb i}} \left(b \frac {{\pmb x}}{\alpha}; \frac {\lambda}{\alpha b ^ {q - 2}}\right).
$$

using the above identity with  $\alpha = 1 + b_0$  and  $b = \alpha \sqrt{d}$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} e _ {q, (1 + b _ {0}) \boldsymbol {i}} \left(\left(1 + b _ {0}\right) ^ {- 1} \left\{\frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {n}} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\}; b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}\right) \\ = \frac {1}{(1 + b _ {0}) d} e _ {q, i} \left(\frac {\sqrt {d} c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1} \sqrt {d}}{2} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \frac {b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}}{(1 + b _ {0}) ^ {q - 1} d ^ {\frac {q}{2} - 1}}\right) \tag {c.3} \\ \end{array}
$$

we next proceed to take the limit of the above expression as  $n\to \infty$  . by assumption 4 we have

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \right\| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q} \rightarrow \sigma_ {m, p} ^ {q} d ^ {\frac {q}{2} - 1}, \quad \left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \rightarrow \sigma_ {m, 2}, \tag {c.4}
$$

with high probability. also by assumption 1, we have  $n / d\to \delta$  . therefore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} e _ {q, (1 + b _ {0}) i} \left(\left(1 + b _ {0}\right) ^ {- 1} \left\{\frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\}; b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}\right) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{(1 + b _ {0}) d} e _ {q, i} \left(\frac {\sqrt {d} c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {n}} p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} h - \frac {c _ {1} \sqrt {d}}{2} \widetilde {\mu}; \frac {b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}}{(1 + b _ {0}) ^ {q - 1} d ^ {\frac {q}{2} - 1}}\right) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{(1 + b _ {0}) d} e _ {q, i} \left(\frac {\sqrt {d} c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \frac {\sqrt {d} \boldsymbol {\mu}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}} ; \frac {b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}}{(1 + b _ {0}) ^ {q - 1} d ^ {\frac {q}{2} - 1}}\right) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{(1 + b _ {0}) d} e _ {q, i} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {\delta}} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \frac {\sqrt {d} \boldsymbol {\mu}}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; b _ {1} (1 + b _ {0}) ^ {1 - q} \sigma_ {m, p} ^ {q}\right) \tag {c.5} \\ \end{array}
$$

we next note that by definition of the weighted moreau envelop we have  $e_{q,\pmb{i}}(\pmb{x};\lambda) = \sum_{i=1}^{d} j_{q}(x_{i};\lambda)$ . also, by assumption 7 the empirical distribution of entries of  $\sqrt{d}\pmb{\mu}$  converges weakly to distribution  $\mathbb{p}_m$ . therefore, continuing from (c.5) we can write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} e _ {q, (1 + b _ {0}) i} \left((1 + b _ {0}) ^ {- 1} \left\{\frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {n}} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\}; b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}\right) \\ \stackrel {(a)} {=} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} e _ {q, (1 + b _ {0}) i} \left(\left(1 + b _ {0}\right) ^ {- 1} \left\{\frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\}; b _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}\right) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{(1 + b _ {0}) d} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {d} j _ {q} \left(\frac {c _ {0} h _ {i}}{2 \sqrt {\delta}} - \frac {c _ {1}}{2} \frac {\sqrt {d} \mu_ {i}}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; b _ {1} (1 + b _ {0}) ^ {1 - q} \sigma_ {m, p} ^ {q}\right) \\ \stackrel {(b)} {=} \frac {1}{1 + b _ {0}} \mathbb {e} \left[ j _ {q} \left(\frac {c _ {0} h}{2 \sqrt {\delta}} - \frac {c _ {1} m}{2 \sigma_ {m , 2}}; b _ {1} (1 + b _ {0}) ^ {1 - q} \sigma_ {m, p} ^ {q}\right) \right] = (1 + b _ {0}) ^ {- 1} \mathcal {j} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{2}, \frac {c _ {1}}{2}; b _ {1} (1 + b _ {0}) ^ {1 - q}\right), \quad (\text {c . 6}) \\ \end{array}
$$

where the expectation in  $(b)$  is taken with respect to the independent random variables  $h\sim \mathsf{n}(0,1)$  and  $m\sim \mathbb{p}_m$ . the last equality follows by definition of function  $\mathcal{i}$  given by (4.7) and by deploying assumption 4.

here,  $(a)$  follows by writing

$$
\frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {\delta}} \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {c _ {1} \sqrt {d} \boldsymbol {\mu}}{2 \sigma_ {m , 2}} = \frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {\delta}} \boldsymbol {h} - \left(\frac {c _ {1} \sqrt {d}}{2 \sigma_ {m , 2}} + \frac {c _ {0}}{2 \sqrt {\delta} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}\right) \boldsymbol {\mu}
$$

and noting that  $\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}^t\pmb {h}\sim \mathsf{n}(0,1)$  since  $\| \widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}\|_{\ell_2} = 1$  , and  $\| \pmb {\mu}\|_{\ell_2}\to \sigma_{m,2}$  which implies that the last term in the right-hand side is dominated by the second term therein that is of order  $\sqrt{d}$

the chain of equalities in (c.6) shows that assumption 6(a) is satisfied by  $f\big(c_0, c_1; b_0, b_1\big) = \mathcal{i}\left(\frac{c_0}{2}, \frac{c_1}{2}; b_1(1 + b_0)^{1 - q}\right)$  for the isotropic model.

assumption 6(b) also clearly holds for isotropic model  $(\pmb{\sigma} = \pmb{i})$  with  $s_{\rho}(z) = \frac{1}{z - 1}$ .

now that assumption 6 holds we can use the result of theorem 6.3 for the special case of  $\pmb{\sigma} = \pmb{i}$ . as we showed above for this case, we have the following identities

$$
f \left(c _ {0}, c _ {1}; b _ {0}, b _ {1}\right) = \mathcal {j} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{2}, \frac {c _ {1}}{2}; b _ {1} (1 + b _ {0}) ^ {1 - q}\right), \quad s _ {\rho} (z) = \frac {1}{z - 1}. \tag {c.7}
$$

now by using these identities in the ao problem (6.2) and after some simple algebraic manipulation we obtain the ao problem (4.8).

# c.2 proof of theorem 4.5

we prove theorem 4.5 as a special case of theorem 6.4. we first show that in the isotropic case, assumption 4 implies assumption 7, required by theorem 6.4.

note that by assumption 4 we have

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \right\| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q} \rightarrow \sigma_ {m, p} ^ {q} d ^ {\frac {q}{2} - 1}, \quad \left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \rightarrow \sigma_ {m, 2}, \tag {c.8}
$$

with high probability.

we then write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} e _ {q, i} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} - c _ {1} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}\right) \stackrel {(a)} {=} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{d} e _ {q, i} \left(\frac {c _ {0} \sqrt {d}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} - c _ {1} \sqrt {d} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \frac {\lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}}{d ^ {\frac {q}{2} - 1}}\right) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{d} e _ {q, i} \left(\frac {c _ {0} \sqrt {d}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} - c _ {1} \frac {\sqrt {d} \boldsymbol {\mu}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}} ; \frac {\lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} ^ {q}}{d ^ {\frac {q}{2} - 1}}\right) \\ \stackrel {(b)} {=} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{d} e _ {q, i} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {\delta}} \boldsymbol {h} - c _ {1} \frac {\sqrt {d} \boldsymbol {\mu}}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m, p} ^ {q}\right) \\ \stackrel {(c)} {=} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{d} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {d} j _ {q} \left(\frac {c _ {0} h _ {i}}{\sqrt {\delta}} - c _ {1} \frac {\sqrt {d} \mu_ {i}}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m, p} ^ {q}\right) \\ \stackrel {(d)} {=} \mathbb {e} \left[ j _ {q} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {\delta}} h - c _ {1} \frac {m}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m, p} ^ {q}\right) \right] \\ = \mathcal {j} \left(c _ {0}, c _ {1}; \lambda_ {0}\right). \tag {c.9} \\ \end{array}
$$

here  $(a)$  follows from (c.2) with  $b = \sqrt{d}$ ;  $(b)$  follows from (c.8);  $(c)$  holds due to the identity  $e_{q,\mathbf{i}}(\pmb{x};\lambda) = \sum_{i=1}^{d} j_q(x_i; \lambda)$ , which follows readily from the definition of weighted moreau envelop  $e_{q,\mathbf{i}}$  and the function  $j_q$  given by (4.5). finally,  $(d)$  holds due to assumption 4. the series of equalities (c.9) implies that assumption 7 holds in isotropic case with

$$
\mathsf {e} \left(c _ {0}, c _ {1}; \lambda_ {0}\right) = \mathcal {j} \left(c _ {0}, c _ {1}; \lambda_ {0}\right). \tag {c.10}
$$

having assumption 6 in place, we can specialize the result of theorem 6.4 to isotropic model. substituting for  $\mathsf{e}(c_0,c_1;\lambda_0)$  from (c.10) in the ao problem (6.4) yields the ao problem (4.16).

# d proofs for special cases of  $p$  (section 5)

# d.1 proof of corollary 5.1

part (a) is already proved in example 2, cf. (4.4).

proof [part (b)] we start by an explicit characterization of  $\mathcal{i}$  function for case of  $p = q = 2$ .

lemma d.1 recall function  $\mathcal{j}(c_0, c_1; \lambda_0)$  given by

$$
\mathcal {j} \left(c _ {0}, c _ {1}; \lambda_ {0}\right) = \mathbb {e} \left[ j _ {q} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {\delta}} h - c _ {1} \frac {m}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m, p} ^ {q}\right) \right], \tag {d.1}
$$

then the following identity holds for case of  $p = q = 2$ :

$$
\mathcal {j} (c _ {0}, c _ {1}; \lambda_ {0}) = \frac {\lambda}{\alpha^ {2} + 2 \lambda \alpha} \| \boldsymbol {x} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}
$$

proof it is straightforward to see that

$$
j _ {2} (x; \lambda) = \frac {\lambda}{1 + 2 \lambda} x ^ {2}.
$$

therefore,

$$
\mathcal {j} (c _ {0}, c _ {1}; \lambda_ {0}) = \frac {\lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{1 + 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {\delta}} h - c _ {1} \frac {m}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}\right) ^ {2} \right] = \frac {\lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{1 + 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \left(\frac {c _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta} + c _ {1} ^ {2}\right).
$$

using lemma d.1 in ao problem (4.8) for  $q = 2$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} d _ {\mathrm {s}} (\alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \theta , \beta , \lambda_ {0}, \eta , \tilde {\eta}) = 2 \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathcal {j} \left(\frac {\beta}{2}, \frac {\tilde {\eta}}{2}; \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{2 \gamma_ {0}} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha}\right) ^ {- 1}\right) \\ - \left(\frac {\beta^ {2}}{\delta} + \tilde {\eta} ^ {2}\right) \frac {1}{4 (1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha})} - \lambda_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \frac {\eta \alpha}{2} - \tilde {\eta} \theta \\ + \beta \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \left((1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m , 2}) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]} \\ = \frac {\frac {\lambda_ {0}}{4 \gamma_ {0}} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{\left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha}\right) ^ {2} + \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha}\right) \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \left(\frac {\beta^ {2}}{\delta} + \tilde {\eta} ^ {2}\right) \\ - \left(\frac {\beta^ {2}}{\delta} + \tilde {\eta} ^ {2}\right) \frac {1}{4 (1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha})} - \lambda_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \frac {\eta \alpha}{2} - \tilde {\eta} \theta \\ + \beta \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m , 2}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]} \\ = - \left(\frac {\beta^ {2}}{\delta} + \tilde {\eta} ^ {2}\right) \frac {1}{4 \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha} + \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}\right)} - \lambda_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \frac {\eta \alpha}{2} - \tilde {\eta} \theta \\ + \beta \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m , 2}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]}. \tag {d.2} \\ \end{array}
$$

setting  $\frac{\partial d_{\mathrm{s}}}{\partial\beta}$  to zero we conclude that

$$
\widehat {\beta} = 2 \delta \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha} + \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2}\right) \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \left((1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m , 2}) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]}.
$$

thus the ao problem reduces to

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min _ {\alpha , \gamma_ {0} \geq 0, \theta} \max _ {\lambda_ {0}, \eta \geq 0, \tilde {\eta}} - \tilde {\eta} ^ {2} \frac {1}{4 (1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha} + \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2})} - \lambda_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \frac {\eta \alpha}{2} - \tilde {\eta} \theta \\ + \delta \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha} + \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2}\right) \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m, 2}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]. \tag {d.3} \\ \end{array}
$$

setting the derivative with respect to  $\widetilde{\eta}$  to zero we arrive at

$$
\widehat {\widetilde {\eta}} = - 2 \theta \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha} + \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2}\right),
$$

which further simplifies the ao problem to

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min _ {\alpha , \gamma_ {0} \geq 0, \theta} \max _ {\lambda_ {0}, \eta \geq 0} - \lambda_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \frac {\eta \alpha}{2} + \theta^ {2} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha} + \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \delta \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \alpha} + \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2}\right) \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m, 2}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]. \tag {d.4} \\ \end{array}
$$

note that if  $\alpha^2 < \theta^2 + \delta \mathbb{e}\left[\left((1 + \varepsilon_0\gamma_0 - \theta \sigma_{m,2}) + \alpha g\right)_+^2\right]$  then the maximum over  $\eta$  is  $+\infty$ . furthermore, when  $\alpha^2 \geq \theta^2 + \delta \mathbb{e}\left[\left((1 + \varepsilon_0\gamma_0 - \theta \sigma_{m,2}) + \alpha g\right)_+^2\right]$  then the optimal  $\eta = 0$ . thus the above ao is equivalent to

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min _ {\alpha , \gamma_ {0} \geq 0, \theta} \max _ {\lambda_ {0} \geq 0} - \lambda_ {0} \gamma_ {0} + \theta^ {2} \left(1 + \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \delta \left(1 + \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2}\right) \mathbb {e} \left[ \left((1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m, 2}) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\text {s u b j e c t} \alpha^ {2} \geq \theta^ {2} + \delta \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m, 2}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right] \tag {d.5}
$$

using a similar argument for optimization over  $\lambda_0$ , it is straightforward to see that the above optimization is equivalent to

$$
\min _ {\alpha , \gamma_ {0} \geq 0, \theta} \theta^ {2} + \delta \mathbb {e} \left[ \left((1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m, 2}) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]
$$

$$
\mathrm {s u b j e c t t o} \quad \alpha^ {2} \geq \theta^ {2} + \delta \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m, 2}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right],
$$

$$
\text {a n d} \quad \frac {\gamma_ {0} ^ {2}}{\sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \geq \theta^ {2} + \delta \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m, 2}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]. \tag {d.6}
$$
