since the objective function is increasing in  $\alpha$  and  $\gamma$ , then the optimal  $\alpha$  and  $\gamma$  should make the inequality constraints equality and therefore  $\gamma_0 = \alpha \sigma_{m,2}$ . this brings us to the following problem:

$$
\min _ {\alpha \geq 0, u} \alpha^ {2}
$$

$$
\text {s u b j e c t} \alpha^ {2} \geq \theta^ {2} + \delta \mathbb {e} \left[ (1 + (\varepsilon_ {0} \alpha - \theta) \sigma_ {m, 2} + \alpha g) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]. \tag {d.7}
$$

by change of variable  $u = \frac{\theta}{\alpha}$  we have

$$
\min _ {\alpha \geq 0, u} \alpha^ {2}
$$

$$
\text {s u b j e c t} \quad 1 \geq u ^ {2} + \delta \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{\alpha} + (\varepsilon_ {0} - u) \sigma_ {m, 2} + g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right] \tag {d.8}
$$

by another change of variable  $\tilde{\alpha} = \left(\frac{1}{\alpha} + \varepsilon_0 \sigma_{m,2}\right)^{-1}$  we have

$$
\min_{\substack{\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{0}\sigma_{m,2}}\geq \tilde{\alpha}\geq 0,u}}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\alpha}} -\varepsilon_{0}\sigma_{m,2}\right)^{-2}
$$

$$
\text {s u b j e c t} \quad 1 \geq u ^ {2} + \delta \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{\tilde {\alpha}} - u \sigma_ {m, 2} + g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right] \tag {d.9}
$$

since objective is increasing in  $\tilde{\alpha}$  this is equivalent to

$$
\min _ {\frac {1}{\varepsilon_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2}} \geq \tilde {\alpha} \geq 0, u} \tilde {\alpha} ^ {2}
$$

$$
\text {s u b j e c t} \quad 1 \geq u ^ {2} + \delta \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{\tilde {\alpha}} - u \sigma_ {m, 2} + g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right] \tag {d.10}
$$

note that we can drop the constraint  $\frac{1}{\varepsilon_0\sigma_{m,2}} \geq \tilde{\alpha}$  because for  $\frac{1}{\varepsilon_0\sigma_{m,2}} = \tilde{\alpha}$  one can already find  $u$  that satisfies the inequality constraint. as such the optimal  $\tilde{\alpha}$  should be less than  $\frac{1}{\varepsilon_0\sigma_{m,2}}$ . to see why, by letting  $u = \frac{\theta}{\sqrt{1 + \theta^2}}$  with  $\theta$  the minimizer in separability condition (4.4) we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} u ^ {2} + \delta \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{\tilde {\alpha}} - u \sigma_ {m, 2} + g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right] = \frac {\theta^ {2}}{1 + \theta^ {2}} + \delta \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(\varepsilon_ {0} - \frac {\theta}{\sqrt {1 + \theta^ {2}}}\right) \sigma_ {m, 2} + g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right] \\ = \frac {\theta^ {2}}{1 + \theta^ {2}} + \frac {\delta}{1 + \theta^ {2}} \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(\sqrt {1 + \theta^ {2}} \varepsilon_ {0} - \theta\right) \sigma_ {m, 2} + \sqrt {1 + \theta^ {2}} g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \frac {\theta^ {2}}{1 + \theta^ {2}} + \frac {1}{1 + \theta^ {2}} = 1. \\ \end{array}
$$

this brings us to the following ao problem:

$$
\min _ {\tilde {\alpha} \geq 0, u} \tilde {\alpha} ^ {2}
$$

$$
\text {s u b j e c t} \quad 1 \geq u ^ {2} + \delta \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{\tilde {\alpha}} - u \sigma_ {m, 2} + g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right] \tag {d.11}
$$

denoting by  $\tilde{\alpha}_{*}$  the solution of the above problem, it is clear that by our change of variable we have

$$
\alpha_ {*} = \left(\tilde {\alpha} _ {*} ^ {- 1} - \varepsilon_ {0} \sigma_ {m, 2}\right) ^ {- 1}, \quad \theta_ {*} = u _ {*} \alpha_ {*}, \quad \gamma_ {0 *} = \alpha_ {*} \sigma_ {m, 2}. \tag {d.12}
$$

this concludes the proof of part (b).

proof [part (c)] we focus on part of the ao problem (4.16) that involves the variables  $\lambda_0, \nu, \tau_h$  and specialize it to the case of  $q = 2$ :

$$
\min _ {\lambda_ {0} \geq 0, \nu} \left[ \frac {\alpha}{\tau_ {h}} \left\{\frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 \delta} + \lambda_ {0} \left(\frac {\gamma_ {0} \tau_ {h}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {2} + \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} - \mathcal {j} \left(\beta , \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \nu\right); \lambda_ {0}\right) \right\} + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2} \right].
$$

we next plug in for  $\mathcal{j}(c_0, c_1; \lambda_0)$  from lemma d.1 which results in

$$
\min _ {\lambda_ {0} \geq 0, \nu} \left[ \frac {\alpha}{\tau_ {h}} \left\{\frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 \delta} + \lambda_ {0} \left(\frac {\gamma_ {0} \tau_ {h}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {2} + \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} - \frac {\lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}}{1 + 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \left(\frac {\beta^ {2}}{\delta} + \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \nu\right) ^ {2}\right) \right\} + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2} \right].
$$

writing the first order optimality for  $\lambda_0$ ,  $\nu$ ,  $\tau_h$  we get a set of equations that admits a solution only if  $\gamma_0 = \sigma_{m,2}\sqrt{\alpha^2 + \theta^2}$ . then,

$$
\nu = 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m, 2} ^ {2} \frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha}, \quad \tau_ {h} = \frac {1}{1 + 2 \lambda_ {0} \sigma_ {m , 2} ^ {2}} \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {\delta}}.
$$

in this case, the value of  $\lambda_0$  does not matter and the above part of the ao simplifies to  $\alpha \beta / \sqrt{\delta}$ . this simplifies the ao problem (4.16) to

$$
\max _ {0 \leq \beta} \min _ {\theta , 0 \leq \alpha , \tau_ {g}} \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + l \left(\sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + \theta^ {2}}, \sigma_ {m, 2} \theta - \varepsilon_ {0} \sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + \theta^ {2}}, \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) - \frac {\alpha \beta}{\sqrt {\delta}}. \tag {d.13}
$$

we next further simplifies the ao problem by solving for  $\tau_{g}$ . we use the shorthand  $l_3'(a,b;\mu) = \frac{\partial l}{\partial\mu} l(a,b;\mu)$  to denote the derivative of the expected moreau envelop with respect to its third argument. writing the first order optimality condition for  $\beta$  and  $\tau_{g}$  in optimization (d.13), we get

$$
\frac {\beta}{2} + \frac {1}{\beta} l _ {3} ^ {\prime} \left(\sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + \theta^ {2}}, \sigma_ {m, 2} \theta - \varepsilon_ {0} \sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + \theta^ {2}}, \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) = 0,
$$

$$
\frac {\tau_ {g}}{2} - \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta^ {2}} l _ {3} ^ {\prime} \left(\sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + \theta^ {2}}, \sigma_ {m, 2} \theta - \varepsilon_ {0} \sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + \theta^ {2}}, \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) - \frac {\alpha}{\sqrt {\delta}} = 0. \tag {d.14}
$$

combining the above two equations, we obtain  $\tau_g = \frac{\alpha}{\sqrt{\delta}}$ . substituting for  $\tau_g$  in (d.13), the ao problem for case of  $q = 2$  simplifies to

$$
\max  _ {0 \leq \beta} \min  _ {\theta , 0 \leq \alpha} l \left(\sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + \theta^ {2}}, \sigma_ {m, 2} \theta - \varepsilon_ {0} \sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + \theta^ {2}}, \frac {\alpha}{\beta \sqrt {\delta}}\right) - \frac {\alpha \beta}{2 \sqrt {\delta}}. \tag {d.15}
$$

this completes the proof of part (c).

# d.2 proof of theorem 5.2

proof [part (a)] the first part of the theorem is on precise characterization of the separability threshold. we use the result of theorem 4.1 that holds for any choice of  $(p,q)$ , in particular  $(p = 1, q = \infty)$ , and relates the separability threshold to the spherical width. what is remaining to prove is the characterization of the spherical width given by (5.9). to this end, we follow a similar argument as in lemma 4.2. however, since  $\| \cdot \|_{\ell_q}^q$  is not well defined for  $q = \infty$  (recall that  $\ell_q$  is the dual norm of  $\ell_p$  and  $p = 1$ ), it requires a slightly different analysis. specifically, in the lagrangian we write the constraint  $\| \boldsymbol{u} \|_{\ell_\infty} \leq \frac{1}{\varepsilon_0 \| \boldsymbol{\mu} \|_{\ell_\infty}}$  as the term  $\| \boldsymbol{u} \|_{\ell_\infty} - \frac{1}{\varepsilon_0 \| \boldsymbol{\mu} \|_{\ell_p}}$ , as compared to the case of finite  $q$  where we raised the both sides to power  $q$  to use the separability property of function  $\| \cdot \|_{\ell_q}^q$ . then, by following a similar derivation as in (e.4), we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {z} \in \mathcal {s} (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}, \boldsymbol {\mu})} - \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {z} \\ = \sup  _ {\lambda , \eta \geq 0, \nu} \eta j _ {\infty} \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {h}}{\eta \sqrt {n}} - \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \frac {\lambda}{\eta}\right) - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2 \eta} - \frac {1}{2 \eta n} \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\nu}{\eta \sqrt {n}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} - \frac {\lambda}{\varepsilon_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {1}}}, \tag {d.16} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $j_{\infty}(\pmb {x};\lambda)$  is defined as

$$
j _ {\infty} (\boldsymbol {x}; \lambda) = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v}} \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {x} - \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {\ell_ {\infty}}. \tag {d.17}
$$

our next step is scalarization of the optimization (d.16) in the large sample limit (as  $n\to \infty$ ), and a challenge along this way is that the function  $j(\pmb {x};\lambda)$  is not a separable function over the entries of  $\pmb{x}$ . to cope with this problem, we propose an alternative representation of this function that involves an additional variable  $t_0$ .

we write

$$
\begin{array}{l} j _ {\infty} (\boldsymbol {x}; \lambda) = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v}} \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {x} - \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {\ell_ {\infty}} \\ = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v}, t \geq 0} \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {x} - \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda t \quad \text {s u b j e c t} \quad \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {\ell_ {\infty}} \leq t \\ = \min  _ {t \geq 0} \frac {1}{2} \| \mathsf {s t} (\boldsymbol {x}; t) \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda t. \tag {d.18} \\ \end{array}
$$

let  $t_0 = \|\pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_1} t$  and  $\lambda_0 = \frac{\lambda}{\|\pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_1}}$ . similar to the trick of 'artificial' boundedness that we used in applying the cgmt framework (e.g., cf. explanation after (b.16) and appendix e.3), we continue by the ansatz that the optimal value of  $\lambda_0$  and  $t_0$  remain bounded as  $n \to \infty$ . after we take the limit of the lagrangian to obtain a scalar auxiliary optimization (ao) problem, this ansatz is verified by the boundedness of solutions of the ao problem.

for  $\pmb{x} = \frac{c_0}{\sqrt{n}} \pmb{h} - c_1 \widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{2} \| \mathsf {s t} (\boldsymbol {x}; t) \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda t \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {d} s t \left(x _ {i}; \frac {t _ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {1}}}\right) ^ {2} + \lambda_ {0} t _ {0} \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {d} \mathsf {s t} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {n}} h _ {i} - c _ {1} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {i}; \frac {t _ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {1}}}\right) ^ {2} + \lambda_ {0} t _ {0} \\ \stackrel {(a)} {=} \lim _ {n \to \infty} \frac {1}{2 d} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {d} \mathsf {s t} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {\delta}} h _ {i} - c _ {1} \frac {\sqrt {d} \mu_ {i}}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \frac {t _ {0}}{\sigma_ {m , 1}}\right) ^ {2} + \lambda_ {0} t _ {0} \\ = \frac {1}{2} \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathsf {s t} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {\delta}} h - c _ {1} \frac {m}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \frac {t _ {0}}{\sigma_ {m , 1}}\right) ^ {2} \right] + \lambda_ {0} t _ {0}, \\ = f \left(c _ {0}, c _ {1}; t _ {0}\right) + \lambda_ {0} t _ {0}, \tag {d.19} \\ \end{array}
$$

with high probability. in  $(a)$  we used the fact that as  $n\to \infty$  , we have  $\| \pmb {\mu}\|_{\ell_1}\rightarrow \sqrt{d}\sigma_{m,1}$  along with the identity  $\frac{1}{a^2}\mathsf{st}(ax;a\lambda) = \mathsf{st}(\pmb {x};\lambda)$

note that this is a pointwise convergence. however, the left hand side is a convex function of  $t$  and it's minimizer satisfies  $t \leq \| \pmb{x} \|_{\infty} \leq c_0 + c_1$  and so belongs to a compact set. therefore, by applying the convexity lemma, see e.g., [lm08, lemma 7.75], [tah18, lemma b1], we can change

the order of limit and minimization, and get that for  $\pmb{x} = \frac{c_0}{\sqrt{n}}\pmb{h} - c_1\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} j _ {\infty} (\boldsymbol {x}; \lambda) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \min  _ {t \geq 0} \left\{\frac {1}{2} \| \mathsf {s t} (\boldsymbol {x}; t) \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda t \right\} \\ = \min  _ {t _ {0} \geq 0} \left\{f \left(c _ {0}, c _ {1}; t _ {0}\right) + \lambda_ {0} t _ {0} \right\}, \tag {d.20} \\ \end{array}
$$

in probability. in addition, as  $n\to \infty$  we have

$$
\frac {1}{n} \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \rightarrow \frac {1}{\delta}, \quad \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} \rightarrow 0, \quad \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {1}} \rightarrow \sigma_ {m, 1} \sqrt {d}, \tag {d.21}
$$

with high probability.

using the above limits, we see that the objective function (d.16) converges pointwise to the following function:

$$
\min  _ {t _ {0} \geq 0} \eta \left\{f \left(\frac {1}{\eta}, \frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta ; t _ {0}\right) + \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\eta} t _ {0} \right\} - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2 \eta} - \frac {1}{2 \eta \delta} - \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} - \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\varepsilon_ {0}} \tag {d.22}
$$

note that (e.4) is the dual optimization and hence is a concave problem. we apply the convexity lemma [tah18, lemma b.2] to conclude that the objective value in (e.4) also converges to the supremum of function (e.8) over  $\lambda_0, \eta \geq 0, \nu$ . therefore the solution of optimization (d.16) converges to the solution of the following optimization problem:

$$
\sup  _ {\lambda_ {0}, \eta \geq 0, \nu} \min  _ {t _ {0} \geq 0} \eta f \left(\frac {1}{\eta}, \frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta ; t _ {0}\right) + \lambda_ {0} t _ {0} - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2 \eta} - \frac {1}{2 \eta \delta} - \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} - \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\varepsilon_ {0}} \tag {d.23}
$$

note that the above objective is linear in  $\lambda_0$ . therefore the optimal  $t_0^*$  should satisfy  $t_0^* \leq \frac{1}{\varepsilon_0}$ . otherwise  $\lambda_0^* = \infty$  which makes the above max-min value unbounded, and this is a contradiction because the above problem involves minimization over  $t_0$  and it is easy to see that by choosing  $t_0 = 0$  the optimal objective value over  $\{\lambda_0, \eta \geq 0, \nu\}$  becomes zero.

therefore, we can assume  $t_0 \leq \frac{1}{\varepsilon_0}$  which yields  $\lambda_0^*(t_0 - \frac{1}{\varepsilon_0}) = 0$ . this simplifies the problem (d.23) to

$$
\sup  _ {\eta \geq 0, \nu} \min  _ {0 \leq t _ {0} \leq \frac {1}{\varepsilon_ {0}}} \eta f \left(\frac {1}{\eta}, \frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta ; t _ {0}\right) - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2 \eta} - \frac {1}{2 \eta \delta} - \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} \tag {d.24}
$$

since  $f(c_0, c_1; t)$  is decreasing in  $t$ , the optimal value  $t_0^*$  is given by  $t_0^* = \frac{1}{\varepsilon_0}$  and the problem is further simplified and along with (d.16) implies that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {z} \in \mathcal {s} (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}, \boldsymbol {\mu})} - \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {z} \\ = \sup  _ {\eta \geq 0, \nu} \eta f \left(\frac {1}{\eta}, \frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta ; \frac {1}{\varepsilon_ {0}}\right) - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2 \eta} - \frac {1}{2 \eta \delta} - \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} \tag {d.25} \\ \end{array}
$$

now similar to the proof of lemma 4.2 we use equation (e.3) to write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \omega (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \omega_ {s} \left(s (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}, \boldsymbol {\mu})\right) \\ = - \sup  _ {\eta \geq 0, \nu} \sqrt {\delta} \left\{\eta f \left(\frac {1}{\eta}, \frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta ; \frac {1}{\varepsilon_ {0}}\right) - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2 \eta} - \frac {1}{2 \eta \delta} - \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} \right\} \\ = \min  _ {\eta \geq 0, \nu} \sqrt {\delta} \left\{\frac {\nu^ {2}}{2 \eta} + \frac {1}{2 \eta \delta} + \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} - \eta f \left(\frac {1}{\eta}, \frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta ; \frac {1}{\varepsilon_ {0}}\right) \right\}. \tag {d.26} \\ \end{array}
$$

this completes the proof.

proof [part (b)] the proof of this parts proceeds along the same lines of theorem 4.3 for the special case of  $p = 1$ ,  $q = \infty$ . however, it requires a slightly different treatment as in part (a) because the function  $\| \cdot \|_{\ell_0}^q$  and therefore  $j_{q}$  given by (4.5) are not well defined in this case.

here we only highlight the modifications that are needed to the proof of theorem 4.3 to apply it for case of  $q = \infty$ .

we proceed the exact same derivation that yields (b.22), repeated here for convenience:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {\theta , \boldsymbol {\theta}, \gamma \geq 0, \alpha \geq 0} \max  _ {\beta , \lambda , \eta \geq 0, \widetilde {\eta}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + 2 \lambda \left(\| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {q}} - \gamma\right) + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \\ + \beta \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon \gamma - \theta \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]} + \eta \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} - \alpha\right) + \widetilde {\eta} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \theta) \tag {d.27} \\ \end{array}
$$

we substitute for  $\left\| \boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{\ell_2}$  using the identity  $\left\| \boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{\ell_2} = \min_{\tau \geq 0}\frac{\left\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{\ell_2}^2}{2\tau} +\frac{\tau}{2}$  to get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {\theta , \boldsymbol {\theta}, \gamma \geq 0, \alpha \geq 0} \max  _ {\beta , \lambda , \eta \geq 0, \widetilde {\eta}} \min  _ {\tau \geq 0} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + 2 \lambda \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {\infty}} - 2 \lambda \gamma + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \mathsf {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \\ + \beta \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon \gamma - \theta \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]} \\ + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\eta \tau}{2} - \eta \alpha + \widetilde {\eta} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \theta). \tag {d.28} \\ \end{array}
$$

specializing it to  $\pmb{\sigma} = \pmb{i}$  and  $q = \infty$ , the optimization over  $\pmb{\theta}$  takes the form

$$
\min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + 2 \lambda \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {\infty}} + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\theta} + \widetilde {\eta} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta}. \tag {d.29}
$$

note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {\tilde {\eta} \boldsymbol {\mu}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}} = \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {\tilde {\eta} \boldsymbol {\mu}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}} - \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \boldsymbol {h} \\ = \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} - \left(\frac {\tilde {\eta}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}} + \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}\right) \boldsymbol {\mu}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}^t\pmb{h} \sim \mathsf{n}(0,1)$  since  $\| \widetilde{\pmb{\mu}} \|_{\ell_2} = 1$ , and  $\| \pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_2} \to \sigma_{m,2}$  which implies that the last term in the right-hand side is dominated by the second term. therefore in the asymptotic regime  $n \to \infty$ , we can equivalently work replace  $\mathsf{p}_{\pmb{\mu}}^{\perp}\pmb{h}$  by  $\pmb{h}$  and by using the symmetry of the gaussian distribution work with

$$
\min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + 2 \lambda \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {\infty}} - \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} + \widetilde {\eta} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta}. \tag {d.30}
$$

we let  $\pmb{x} = \frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\pmb{h} - \tilde{\eta}\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$  and consider the change of variable  $\pmb{u} := 2(1 + \frac{\eta}{2\tau})\pmb{\theta}$  and write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + 2 \lambda \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {\infty}} - \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} + \tilde {\eta} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} \\ = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} \frac {1}{2} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) ^ {- 1} \left\{\frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + 2 \lambda \| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {\ell_ {\infty}} - \boldsymbol {x} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {u} \right\} \\ = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} \frac {1}{2} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) ^ {- 1} \left\{\frac {1}{2} \left\| \boldsymbol {u} - \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + 2 \lambda \left\| \boldsymbol {u} \right\| _ {\ell_ {\infty}} - \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}}{2} \right\} \\ = \frac {1}{2} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) ^ {- 1} j _ {\infty} (\boldsymbol {x}; 2 \lambda) - \frac {1}{4} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) ^ {- 1} \| \boldsymbol {x} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \tag {d.31} \\ \end{array}
$$

using (d.31) and substituting for  $\pmb{x}$  in (d.27), our ao problem becomes

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min_{\theta ,\gamma \geq 0,\alpha \geq 0}\max_{\beta ,\lambda ,\eta \geq 0,\widetilde{\eta}}\min_{\tau \geq 0} \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \frac{\eta}{2\tau}\right)^{-1}j_{\infty}\left(\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol {h} - \widetilde{\eta}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mu}};2\lambda\right) - \frac{1}{4}\left(1 + \frac{\eta}{2\tau}\right)^{-1}\left\| \frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol {h} - \widetilde{\eta}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2} \\ - 2 \lambda \gamma + \beta \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon \gamma - \theta \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]} + \frac {\eta \tau}{2} - \eta \alpha - \widetilde {\eta} \theta \tag {d.32} \\ \end{array}
$$

our next step is to scalarize the ao problem by taking the asymptotic limit of the objective.

we have

$$
\lim  _ {n \to \infty} \left\| \frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} - \tilde {\eta} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} = \frac {\beta^ {2}}{\delta} + \tilde {\eta} ^ {2}, \quad \lim  _ {n \to \infty} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} = \sigma_ {m, 2}.
$$

in probability. also by using (d.20) we have

$$
\lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} j _ {\infty} \left(\frac {\beta}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} - \tilde {\eta} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; 2 \lambda\right) = \min  _ {t _ {0} \geq 0} \left\{f (\beta , \tilde {\eta}; t _ {0}) + 2 \lambda_ {0} t _ {0} \right\},
$$

with  $\lambda_0 = \frac{\lambda}{\|\pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_1}}$ . using these limits in the ao problem (d.32) we obtain the following scalar ao problem

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min_{\theta ,\gamma \geq 0,\alpha \geq 0}\max_{\beta ,\lambda ,\eta \geq 0,\widetilde{\eta}}\min_{\tau ,t_{0}\geq 0} \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \frac{\eta}{2\tau}\right)^{-1}\left(f\big(\beta ,\widetilde{\eta};t_{0}\big) + 2\lambda_{0}t_{0}\right) - \frac{1}{4}\left(1 + \frac{\eta}{2\tau}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\delta} +\widetilde{\eta}^{2}\right) \\ - 2 \lambda_ {0} \gamma_ {0} + \beta \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m , 2}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]} + \frac {\eta \tau}{2} - \eta \alpha - \widetilde {\eta} \theta , \tag {d.33} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we recall our notation  $\varepsilon_0 = \frac{\varepsilon}{\|\pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_1}}$  and  $\gamma_0 = \gamma \left\| \pmb {\mu}\right\|_{\ell_1}$

now note that objective function (d.33) is linear in  $\lambda_0$  and therefore the optimal  $t_0^*$  should satisfy  $t_0^* \leq 2\gamma_0\left(1 + \frac{\eta}{2\tau}\right)$ , otherwise  $\lambda_0^* = \infty$  which makes the above max-min value unbounded. as such, we also have  $\lambda_0^* = 0$  which further simplifies the problem as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min _ {\theta , \gamma \geq 0, \alpha \geq 0} \max _ {\beta , \eta \geq 0, \widetilde {\eta}} \min _ {0 \leq \tau , 0 \leq t _ {0} \leq 2 \gamma_ {0} (1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau})} \frac {1}{2} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) ^ {- 1} f (\beta , \widetilde {\eta}; t _ {0}) - \frac {1}{4} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\frac {\beta^ {2}}{\delta} + \widetilde {\eta} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \beta \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m , 2}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]} + \frac {\eta \tau}{2} - \eta \alpha - \widetilde {\eta} \theta , \tag {d.34} \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $f(c_0, c_1; t_0)$  is decreasing in  $t_0$ , the optimal value of  $t_0$  is given by  $t_0^* = 2\gamma_0\left(1 + \frac{\eta}{2\tau}\right)$  which results in the following ao problem:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min _ {\theta , \gamma \geq 0, \alpha \geq 0} \max _ {\beta , \eta \geq 0, \widetilde {\eta}} \min _ {\tau \geq 0} \frac {1}{2} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) ^ {- 1} f \left(\beta , \widetilde {\eta}; 2 \gamma_ {0} \big (1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau} \big)\right) - \frac {1}{4} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\frac {\beta^ {2}}{\delta} + \widetilde {\eta} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \beta \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m , 2}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]} + \frac {\eta \tau}{2} - \eta \alpha - \widetilde {\eta} \theta . \tag {d.35} \\ \end{array}
$$

our final step of simplification is to solve for  $\tau$ . to this end, we define the function

$$
r \left(\frac {\eta}{\tau}\right) := \frac {1}{2} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) ^ {- 1} f \left(\beta , \tilde {\eta}; 2 \gamma_ {0} \big (1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau} \big)\right) - \frac {1}{4} \left(1 + \frac {\eta}{2 \tau}\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\frac {\beta^ {2}}{\delta} + \tilde {\eta} ^ {2}\right),
$$

where we make the dependence on  $\frac{\eta}{\tau}$  explicit in the notation. setting derivative of the ao objective with respect to  $\eta$ , to zero we obtain

$$
\frac {1}{\tau} r ^ {\prime} \left(\frac {\eta}{\tau}\right) + \frac {\tau}{2} - \alpha = 0.
$$

setting derivative with respect to  $\tau$  to zero gives

$$
- \frac {\eta}{\tau^ {2}} r ^ {\prime} \left(\frac {\eta}{\tau}\right) + \frac {\eta}{2} = 0.
$$

combining the above two optimality conditions implies that  $\eta(1 - \frac{\alpha}{\tau}) = 0$ . so either  $\alpha = \tau$  or  $\eta = 0$ . if  $\eta = 0$ , then it is clear that the terms involving  $\tau$  in the ao problem would vanish and therefore the value of  $\tau$  does not matter. so in this case, we can as well assume  $\tau = \alpha$ . substituting for  $\tau$  the ao problem further simplifies to

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min_{\theta ,\gamma \geq 0,\alpha \geq 0}\max_{\beta ,\eta \geq 0,\widetilde{\eta}}\quad \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \frac{\eta}{2\alpha}\right)^{-1}f\left(\beta ,\widetilde{\eta};2\gamma_{0}\bigl(1 + \frac{\eta}{2\alpha}\bigr)\right) - \frac{1}{4}\left(1 + \frac{\eta}{2\alpha}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\delta} +\widetilde{\eta}^{2}\right) \\ + \beta \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(1 + \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0} - \theta \sigma_ {m , 2}\right) + \alpha g\right) _ {+} ^ {2} \right]} - \frac {\eta \tau}{2} - \widetilde {\eta} \theta . \tag {d.36} \\ \end{array}
$$

this concludes the proof of part (b).

proof [part (c)] the proof of part (c) follows along the same lines of the proof of theorem 6.4 (and theorem 4.5 for isotropic case). but similar to previous parts, we need to make slight modifications to the proof.

note that in our derivation of the ao problem (6.9), we replaced the constraint  $\| \pmb{u}\|_{\ell_q} \leq \frac{\tau_h}{\alpha}\frac{\gamma_0}{\|\pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_p}}$  with the equivalent constraint  $\| \pmb{u}\|_{\ell_q}^q \leq (\frac{\tau_h}{\alpha}\frac{\gamma_0}{\|\pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_p}})^q$ , see (e.11) for more details. the benefit of this alternative representation is that it results in the moreau-envelope  $e_{q,\sigma}$  of the  $\| \cdot \|_{\ell_q}^q$  function, see (e.15), which is separable over the samples. as a result, in the isotropic case the expected moreau envelope reduces to the expected of the one-dimensional function  $j_{q}$ , given by (4.5), (c.9).

however, for  $q = \infty$  the function  $\| \cdot \|_{\ell_q}^q$  is not well-defined and requires a slightly different treatment. in this case we stay with the original constraint  $\| \pmb{u}\|_{\ell_q}\leq \frac{\tau_h}{\alpha}\frac{\gamma_0}{\|\pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_p}}$ . proceeding along the same derivations of ao problem (4.16), it is straightforward to see that this results in the following ao problem for the non-separable regime:

$$
\max  _ {0 \leq \beta , \tau_ {h}} \min  _ {\theta , 0 \leq \alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \tau_ {g}} d _ {\mathrm {n s}} (\alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \theta , \tau_ {g}, \beta , \tau_ {h})
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} d _ {\mathrm {n s}} \left(\alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \theta , \tau_ {g}, \beta , \tau_ {h}\right) = \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + l \left(\sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + \theta^ {2}}, \sigma_ {m, 2} \theta - \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0}, \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) \\ - \min _ {\lambda_ {0} \geq 0, \nu} \left[ \frac {\alpha}{\tau_ {h}} \left\{\frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 \delta} + \lambda_ {0} \frac {\gamma_ {0} \tau_ {h}}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} - \tilde {\mathsf {e}} \left(\beta , \frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \nu ; \lambda_ {0}\right) \right\} + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2} \right] \tag {d.37} \\ \end{array}
$$

with

$$
\tilde {\mathsf {e}} \left(c _ {0}, c _ {1}; \lambda_ {0}\right) := \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} j _ {\infty} \left(\frac {c _ {0}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} - c _ {1} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \lambda_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {1}}\right), \tag {d.38}
$$

and  $j_{\infty}(\pmb {x};\lambda)$  given by (d.17). using (d.20), we have

$$
\tilde {\mathrm {e}} \left(c _ {0}, c _ {1}; \lambda_ {0}\right) = \min  _ {t _ {0} \geq 0} \left\{f \left(c _ {0}, c _ {1}; t _ {0}\right) + \lambda_ {0} t _ {0} \right\}, \tag {d.39}
$$

substituting for  $\tilde{\mathsf{e}}$  function in the ao problem (d.37) results in

$$
\max  _ {0 \leq \beta , \tau_ {h}} \min  _ {\theta , 0 \leq \alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \tau_ {g}} d _ {\mathrm {n s}} (\alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \theta , \tau_ {g}, \beta , \tau_ {h})
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} d _ {\mathrm {n s}} \left(\alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \theta , \tau_ {g}, \beta , \tau_ {h}\right) = \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + l \left(\sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + \theta^ {2}}, \sigma_ {m, 2} \theta - \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0}, \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) \\ - \min  _ {\lambda_ {0} \geq 0, \nu} \sup  _ {t _ {0} \geq 0} \left[ \frac {\alpha}{\tau_ {h}} \left\{\frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 \delta} + \lambda_ {0} \left(\frac {\gamma_ {0} \tau_ {h}}{\alpha} - t _ {0}\right) + \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} - f \left(\beta , \frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \nu ; t _ {0}\right) \right\} + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2} \right] (d. 4 0) \\ \end{array}
$$

note that the above objective is linear in  $\lambda_0$ . clearly, the optimal value  $t_0^*$  should satisfy  $t_0^* \leq \frac{\gamma_0 \tau_h}{\alpha}$ ; otherwise  $\lambda_0^* = \infty$  which makes the objective value unbounded. for  $t_0 \leq \frac{\gamma_0 \tau_h}{\alpha}$ , we have  $\lambda_0^* = 0$ . therefore,  $t_0$  only appears in the term  $f(\beta, \frac{\tau_h \theta}{\alpha} + \nu; t_0)$ . given that  $f(c_0, c_1; t_0)$  is decreasing in  $t_0$ , we have  $t_0^* = \frac{\gamma_0 \tau_h}{\alpha}$ .

we substitute for  $t_0^*$  in the ao problem to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {0 \leq \beta , \tau_ {h}} \min  _ {\theta , 0 \leq \alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \tau_ {g}} d _ {\mathrm {n s}} (\alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \theta , \tau_ {g}, \beta , \tau_ {h}) \\ d _ {\mathrm {n s}} (\alpha , \gamma_ {0}, \theta , \tau_ {g}, \beta , \tau_ {h}) = \frac {\beta \tau_ {g}}{2} + l \left(\sqrt {\alpha^ {2} + \theta^ {2}}, \sigma_ {m, 2} \theta - \varepsilon_ {0} \gamma_ {0}, \frac {\tau_ {g}}{\beta}\right) \\ - \min  _ {\nu} \left[ \frac {\alpha}{\tau_ {h}} \left\{\frac {\beta^ {2}}{2 \delta} + \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} - f \left(\beta , \frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \nu ; \frac {\gamma_ {0} \tau_ {h}}{\alpha}\right) \right\} + \frac {\alpha \tau_ {h}}{2} \right]. \tag {d.41} \\ \end{array}
$$

this completes the proof of part (c).

# proof of technical lemmas

# e.1 proof of lemma 2.1

by definition, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {s a} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}}) := \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbb {1} (\hat {y} = y) \right] = \mathbb {p} (y \langle \boldsymbol {x}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \rangle > 0) \\ = \mathbb {p} \left(y \langle y \boldsymbol {\mu} + \sigma^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {z}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \rangle > 0\right) \\ = \mathbb {p} \left(\langle \boldsymbol {\mu} + \sigma^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {z}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \rangle > 0\right) \\ = \mathbb {p} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\mu}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\rangle + \left\| \sigma^ {1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} z > 0\right) \\ = \phi \left(\frac {\langle \boldsymbol {\mu} , \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \rangle}{\left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}}}\right), \tag {e.1} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\mathbf{z} \sim \mathsf{n}(0, \mathbf{i}_d)$  and  $z \sim \mathsf{n}(0, 1)$ .

likewise for the adversarial risk we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {r a} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}}) := \mathbb {e} \left[ \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {\delta} \| _ {\ell_ {p}} \leq \varepsilon} \mathbb {1} (y \langle \boldsymbol {x} + \boldsymbol {\delta}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \rangle \geq 0) \right] \\ \stackrel {(a)} {=} \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbb {1} \left(y \langle \boldsymbol {x}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \rangle - \varepsilon \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {\ell_ {q}} \geq 0\right) \right] \\ = \mathbb {p} \left(y \langle \boldsymbol {x}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \rangle - \varepsilon \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {\ell_ {q}} \geq 0\right) \\ = \mathbb {p} \left(y \langle y \boldsymbol {\mu} + \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {z}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \rangle - \varepsilon \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {\ell_ {q}} \geq 0\right) \\ \stackrel {(b)} {=} \mathbb {p} \left(\langle \boldsymbol {\mu}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \rangle + \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} z - \varepsilon \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {q}} \geq 0\right) \\ = \phi \left(\frac {\left\langle \boldsymbol {\mu} , \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\rangle - \varepsilon \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {q}}}{\left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}}}\right), \tag {e.2} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $(a)$  we used that  $\langle \delta, \widehat{\pmb{\theta}} \rangle \geq -\|\pmb{\delta}\|_{\ell_p} \left\| \widehat{\pmb{\theta}} \right\|_{\ell_q} \geq -\varepsilon \left\| \widehat{\pmb{\theta}} \right\|_{\ell_q}$ , using hölder inequality (with  $\frac{1}{p} + \frac{1}{q} = 1$ ) and that  $\|\pmb{\delta}\|_{\ell_p} \leq \varepsilon$ , with equality achieving for some  $\pmb{\delta}$  in this set. in  $(b)$ , we used the symmetry of gaussian distribution.

# e.2 proof of lemma 4.2

we first note that

$$
\min  _ {\boldsymbol {z} \in \mathcal {s} (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}, \boldsymbol {\mu})} - \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {z} = - \frac {1}{\sqrt {n / d}} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {z} \in \mathcal {s} (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}, \boldsymbol {\mu})} \frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {z} \rightarrow - \frac {1}{\sqrt {\delta}} \omega (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}), \tag {e.3}
$$

in probability, using the fact that  $\pmb{h} / \sqrt{d}$  is asymptotically uniform on the unit sphere, and for  $s \in \mathbb{s}^{d-1}$  the function  $f(\pmb{u}) = \sup_{\pmb{z} \in s} \pmb{z}^t \pmb{u}$  is lipschitz. therefore, using the concentration of lipschitz

functions on the sphere (see e.g. [ver18, theorem 5.2.2]),  $f(\pmb{u})$  concentrates around its mean  $\mathbb{e}f(\pmb{u}) = \omega_s(s(\alpha ,\theta ,\varepsilon_0,\pmb {\mu}))$ . more precisely,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left\{\left| \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {z} \in \mathcal {s}} \frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {z} - \omega_ {s} (\mathcal {s} (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}, \boldsymbol {\mu})) \right| \right\} \leq 2 e ^ {- c d t ^ {2}},
$$

for an absolute constant  $c > 0$  and for every  $t \geq 0$ . therefore, by invoking the assumption on the convergence of spherical width, cf. assumption 3, we arrive at

$$
\lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb {p} \left\{\left| \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {z} \in \mathcal {s}} \frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {z} - \omega (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}) \right| \geq \eta \right\} = 0, \quad \forall \eta > 0.
$$

therefore,  $\sup_{\pmb {z}\in \mathcal{s}(\alpha ,\theta ,\varepsilon_0)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\pmb{h}^t\pmb {z}\to \omega (\alpha ,\theta ,\varepsilon_0)$  , in probability.

to evaluate the left hand side, we form the lagrangian corresponding to the set  $s$ . let  $\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}} \coloneqq \frac{\pmb{\mu}}{\|\pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_2}}$  and consider the change of variable  $\pmb{u} \coloneqq \pmb{z} + \theta \widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$ . we then have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {z} \in \mathcal {s} (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}, \boldsymbol {\mu})} - \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {z} \\ = \sup  _ {\lambda , \eta \geq 0, \nu} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} - \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {t} (\boldsymbol {u} - \theta \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}) + \lambda \left(\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {\ell_ {q}} ^ {q} - \left(\frac {1}{\varepsilon_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}}\right) ^ {q}\right) + \frac {\eta}{2} \left(\| \boldsymbol {u} - \theta \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} - \alpha^ {2}\right) + \nu \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} (\boldsymbol {u} - \theta \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}) \\ = \sup  _ {\lambda , \eta \geq 0, \nu} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} \frac {\eta}{2} \left\| \boldsymbol {u} - \theta \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} + \frac {\nu}{\eta} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {h}}{\eta \sqrt {n}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda \| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {\ell_ {q}} ^ {q} - \frac {1}{2 \eta} \left\| \nu \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {h}}{\sqrt {n}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} - \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} - \frac {\lambda}{(\varepsilon_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}) ^ {q}} \\ = \sup  _ {\lambda , \eta \geq 0, \nu} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} \eta \left[ \frac {1}{2} \left\| \boldsymbol {u} + \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {h}}{\eta \sqrt {n}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\eta} \| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {\ell_ {q}} ^ {q} \right] - \frac {1}{2 \eta} \left\| \nu \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {h}}{\sqrt {n}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} - \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} - \frac {\lambda}{\left(\varepsilon_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}\right) ^ {q}} \\ = \sup  _ {\lambda , \eta \geq 0, \nu} \eta \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {d} j _ {q} \left(\frac {h _ {i}}{\eta \sqrt {n}} - \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \tilde {\mu} _ {i}; \frac {\lambda}{\eta}\right) - \frac {1}{2 \eta} \left\| \nu \tilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {h}}{\sqrt {n}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} - \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} - \frac {\lambda}{(\varepsilon_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}) ^ {q}} \\ = \sup  _ {\lambda , \eta \geq 0, \nu} \eta \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {d} j _ {q} \left(\frac {h _ {i}}{\eta \sqrt {n}} - \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \tilde {\mu} _ {i}; \frac {\lambda}{\eta}\right) - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2 \eta} - \frac {1}{2 \eta n} \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\nu}{\eta \sqrt {n}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} - \frac {\lambda}{\left(\varepsilon_ {0} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}\right) ^ {q}} \tag {e.4} \\ \end{array}
$$

recall that  $\pmb{h} \sim \mathsf{n}(0, \pmb{i}_d)$ . as  $n \to \infty$  and  $n / d \to \delta$ , we have

$$
\frac {1}{n} \left\| \boldsymbol {h} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \rightarrow \frac {1}{\delta}, \quad \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} \rightarrow 0, \tag {e.5}
$$

in probability. in addition,

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \right\| _ {\ell_ {p}} \rightarrow \sigma_ {m, p} d ^ {\frac {1}{p} - \frac {1}{2}} = \sigma_ {m, p} d ^ {\frac {1}{2} - \frac {1}{q}}, \tag {e.6}
$$

in probability. using the identity  $j_{q}(x;\lambda) = c^{2}j_{q}\big(x / c;\lambda c^{q - 2}\big)$  and letting  $\lambda_0\coloneqq \lambda d^{1 - \frac{q}{2}}$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {d} j _ {q} \left(\frac {h _ {i}}{\eta \sqrt {n}} - \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \tilde {\mu} _ {i}; \frac {\lambda}{\eta}\right) = \frac {1}{d} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {d} j _ {q} \left(\frac {\sqrt {d} h _ {i}}{\eta \sqrt {n}} - \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \frac {\sqrt {d} \mu_ {i}}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \frac {\lambda}{\eta} d ^ {1 - q / 2}\right) \\ = \frac {1}{d} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {d} j _ {q} \left(\frac {h _ {i}}{\eta \sqrt {\delta}} - \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \frac {\sqrt {d} \mu_ {i}}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\eta}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $j_{q}(x;\lambda) \leq \frac{1}{2} x^{2}$ , the function  $j_{q}$  is pseudo-lipschitz of order 2 and by an application of [bm11, lemma 5], we have

$$
\lim _ {n \to \infty} \frac {1}{d} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {d} j _ {q} \left(\frac {h _ {i}}{\eta \sqrt {\delta}} - \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \frac {\sqrt {d} \mu_ {i}}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\eta}\right) = \mathbb {e} \left[ j _ {q} \left(\frac {h}{\eta \sqrt {\delta}} - \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \frac {m}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\eta}\right) \right], \tag {e.7}
$$

almost surely, where the expectation in the last line is taken with respect to the independent random variables  $h \sim \mathsf{n}(0,1)$  and  $m \sim \mathbb{p}_m$ .

using the above limits, we see that the objective function (e.4) converges pointwise to the following function:

$$
\eta \mathbb {e} \left[ j _ {q} \left(\frac {h}{\eta \sqrt {\delta}} - \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \frac {m}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\eta}\right) \right] - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2 \eta} - \frac {1}{2 \eta \delta} - \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} - \lambda_ {0} (\varepsilon_ {0} \sigma_ {m, p}) ^ {- q} \tag {e.8}
$$

note that (e.4) is the dual optimization and hence is a concave problem. we apply the convexity lemma [tah18, lemma b.2] to conclude that the objective value in (e.4) also converges to the supremum of function (e.8) over  $\lambda_0, \eta \geq 0, \nu$ .

using this observation along with equation (e.3) and (e.4) we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \omega (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}) \\ = \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \omega_ {s} \left(s (\alpha , \theta , \varepsilon_ {0}, \boldsymbol {\mu})\right) \\ = - \sup _ {\lambda_ {0}, \eta \geq 0, \nu} \eta \sqrt {\delta} \mathbb {e} \left[ j _ {q} \left(\frac {h}{\eta \sqrt {\delta}} - \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \frac {m}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\eta}\right) \right] - \sqrt {\delta} \left\{\frac {\nu^ {2}}{2 \eta} + \frac {1}{2 \eta \delta} + \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} + \lambda_ {0} (\varepsilon_ {0} \sigma_ {m, p}) ^ {- q} \right\} \\ = \min  _ {\lambda_ {0}, \eta \geq 0, \nu} \sqrt {\delta} \left\{\frac {\nu^ {2}}{2 \eta} + \frac {1}{2 \eta \delta} + \frac {\eta}{2} \alpha^ {2} + \lambda_ {0} \left(\varepsilon_ {0} \sigma_ {m, p}\right) ^ {- q} \right\} - \eta \sqrt {\delta} \mathbb {e} \left[ j _ {q} \left(\frac {h}{\eta \sqrt {\delta}} - \left(\frac {\nu}{\eta} - \theta\right) \frac {m}{\sigma_ {m , 2}}; \frac {\lambda_ {0}}{\eta}\right) \right], \tag {e.9} \\ \end{array}
$$

which completes the proof.

# e.3 proofs that the minimization and maximization primal problems can be restricted to a compact set

in this section we demonstrate how the minimization and maximization problems can be restricted to compacts sets.

# e.3.1 bounded domains in optimization (b.17)

we start with the restriction on  $\pmb{\theta}$ . note that one of the claims of theorem 6.3, part (b), is to show that  $\left\| \widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}^{\varepsilon} \right\|_{\ell_2} \to \alpha_*$  as  $n \to \infty$ , in probability, for some  $\alpha_*$  by the solution of minimax problem (6.2). we define  $\mathcal{s}_{\pmb{\theta}} = \{\pmb{\theta} : \| \pmb{\theta} \|_{\ell_2} \leq k_{\alpha}\}$  with  $k_{\alpha} = \alpha_* + \xi$  for a constant  $\xi > 0$ . we start by the ansatz that  $\pmb{\theta} \in \mathcal{s}_{\pmb{\theta}}$  and add this 'artificial constraint' in the minimax optimization. in addition, by the stationary condition for  $\gamma$  in optimization (b.17) we have

$$
\frac {1}{n} \varepsilon \mathbf {u} ^ {t} \mathbf {1} = 2 \lambda .
$$

therefore

$$
\frac {1}{n} \pmb {u} ^ {t} \pmb {1} = \frac {2 \lambda}{\varepsilon} = \frac {2 \lambda}{\varepsilon_ {0} \| \pmb {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}}.
$$

let  $\lambda_0 \coloneqq \frac{\lambda}{\|\pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_p}}$ . assuming the ansatz that  $\lambda_0 = o(1)$ , we also use this 'artificial constraint' in the minimax optimization.

with these compact constraints in place, we then deploy the cgmt framework to prove theorem 6.3. this theorem implies that  $\left\| \widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\ell_2} \to \alpha_*$  as  $n \to \infty$  and so our initial ansatz on the boundedness of  $\pmb{\theta}$  is verified. further, as it can be seen from the proof of theorem 6.3 (see the line following equation (b.28)), we have  $\lambda_0 = \frac{\lambda}{\|\pmb{\mu}\|_{\ell_p}} \to \lambda_{0*}$  as  $n \to \infty$ , in probability, for some  $\lambda_{0*}$  that is determined by the solution of minimax problem (6.2). this also verifies our ansatz that  $\lambda_0 = o(1)$  which in turn implies that  $\mathbf{u} \in s_{\mathbf{u}} = \{\mathbf{u}: 0 \leq u_i, \frac{1}{n}\mathbf{1}^t\mathbf{u} \leq k_{\mathbf{u}}\}$  for some sufficiently large constant  $k_{\mathbf{u}} > 0$ .

# e.3.2 bounded domains in optimization (b.38)

similar to previous subsection, we start by the ansatz that  $\pmb{\theta} \in s_{\pmb{\theta}}$  where  $s_{\pmb{\theta}} = \{\pmb{\theta} : \| \pmb{\theta}\|_{\ell_2} \leq k_{\alpha}\}$  with  $k_{\alpha} = \alpha_* + \xi$  for a constant  $\xi > 0$ , and add this 'artificial constraint' in the minimax optimization (b.38). also by stationarity condition for  $\pmb{v}$  in (b.38) we have  $u_i = \ell' \left(v_i - \varepsilon \| \pmb{\theta}\|_{\ell_q}\right)$  and hence  $|u_i| \leq k_{\pmb{u}}$  for some large enough constant  $k_{\pmb{u}} > 0$ , using our assumption on the loss function  $\ell$ .

# e.4 proof of lemma b.1

first note that by cauchy-schwarz inequality we have

$$
\left\langle \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {r}, \left(\mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp}\right) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp} \right\rangle \geq - \left\| \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {r} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} \left\| \left(\mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp}\right) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\perp} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} = - \alpha \left\| \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {r} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}}.
$$

to achieve equality, note that similar to (b.50) we have

$$
\left(\mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp}\right) \left(\mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \sigma^ {- 1 / 2} \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp}\right) \boldsymbol {r} = \mathsf {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {r}.
$$

therefore equality is achieved by choosing  $\pmb{\theta} = \lambda \pmb{\sigma}^{-1/2} \mathsf{p}_{\pmb{\mu}}^{\perp} \pmb{r}$  with  $\lambda = \frac{\alpha}{\left\| \mathsf{p}_{\pmb{\mu}}^{\perp} \pmb{r} \right\|_{\ell_2}}$ .

# e.5 proof of lemma b.2

by definition of the conjugate function we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\ell}} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {w}) = \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \ell (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {\theta}) \\ = \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\theta} - \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(v _ {i} - \varepsilon \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {q}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

now assume  $\pmb{\theta} = \gamma \pmb{u}$  with  $\| \pmb{u}\|_{\ell_q} = 1$ . we thus have,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\ell}} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {w}) = \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {u}: \| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {\ell_ {q}} = 1, \gamma} \gamma \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {u} - \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell (v _ {i} - \varepsilon \gamma) \\ = \sup  _ {\gamma \geq 0} \quad \gamma \left(\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {u}: \| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {\ell_ {q}} = 1} \boldsymbol {w} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {u}\right) - \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(v _ {i} - \varepsilon \gamma\right) \\ = \sup  _ {\gamma \geq 0} \quad \gamma \left\| \boldsymbol {w} \right\| _ {\ell_ {p}} - \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(v _ {i} - \varepsilon \gamma\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

# e.6 proof of lemma b.3

by definition

$$
\begin{array}{l} f ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {u}) := \sup  _ {\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}} \langle \boldsymbol {u}, \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \rangle - f (\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}) \\ = \sup  _ {\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}} \left\langle \boldsymbol {u}, \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\rangle + \left\langle \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\rangle \frac {\theta \tau_ {h}}{\alpha} - \frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {p}} \\ = \sup  _ {\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}} \left\langle \boldsymbol {u} + \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \frac {\theta \tau_ {h}}{\alpha}, \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\rangle - \frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {p}} \\ = \sup  _ {\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}} \left\langle \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {u} + \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \frac {\theta \tau_ {h}}{\alpha}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\rangle - \frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {p}} \\ \end{array}
$$

by hölder's inequality,

$$
\left\langle \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {u} + \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \frac {\theta \tau_ {h}}{\alpha}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\rangle \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {u} + \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \frac {\theta \tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \right\| _ {\ell_ {q}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {p}}
$$

therefore, if  $\pmb{u} \in s$  then the supremum is achieved by choosing  $\tilde{\pmb{w}} = 0$ . if  $\pmb{u} \notin s$ , by scaling  $\tilde{\pmb{w}}$  the supremum would be  $+\infty$ .

# e.7 proof of lemma b.4

fix arbitrary  $\pmb{u}$ . by definition,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} (\boldsymbol {u}) := \arg \min  _ {\boldsymbol {z} \in \mathcal {b}} \| \boldsymbol {u} - \boldsymbol {z} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} \\ = \arg \min  _ {\boldsymbol {z} \in \mathcal {b}} \left\| p _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} (\boldsymbol {u} - \boldsymbol {z}) \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \left\| p _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} (\boldsymbol {u} - \boldsymbol {z}) \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(a)} {=} \arg \min  _ {\boldsymbol {z} \in \mathcal {b}} \left\| \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {u} - \boldsymbol {z} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \left\| \mathrm {p} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \boldsymbol {u} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \\ = \arg \min  _ {\boldsymbol {z} \in \mathcal {b}} \left\| p _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {u} - \boldsymbol {z} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \\ = \mathrm {p} _ {\mathcal {b}} \mathrm {p} _ {\mu} ^ {\perp} \boldsymbol {u}, \tag {e.10} \\ \end{array}
$$

where step  $(a)$  follows from that fact that  $z\in \mathcal{b}$  and hence  $z = \mathsf{p}_{\mu}^{\perp}z$

# e.8 proof of lemma b.5

by definition of the set  $\mathcal{b}$ , the value of  $\| \mathsf{p}_{\mathcal{b}}(\pmb {h}) - \pmb {h}\|_{\ell_2}^2$  is given by the optimal objective value of the following optimization:

$$
\operatorname {m i n i m i z e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \quad \| \boldsymbol {z} - \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}
$$

$$
\text {s u b j e c t} \quad \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {z} - \frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {q}} \leq \frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}}, \quad \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {z} = 0. \tag {e.11}
$$

by the change of variable  $\pmb{u} \coloneqq \pmb{\sigma}^{-1/2}\pmb{z} - \frac{\tau_h\theta}{\alpha}\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$  and forming the lagrangian, the optimal value of (e.11) is equal to the optimal value of the following problem:

$$
\sup  _ {\lambda \geq 0, \nu} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} \frac {1}{2} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\boldsymbol {u} + \frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}\right) - \boldsymbol {h} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {u} \right\| _ {\ell_ {q}} ^ {q} - \left(\frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}}\right) ^ {q}\right) + \nu \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\boldsymbol {u} + \frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}\right). \tag {e.12}
$$

rearranging the terms we get the next alternative representation

$$
\sup  _ {\lambda \geq 0, \nu} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} \frac {1}{2} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\boldsymbol {u} + \frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}\right) - \boldsymbol {h} + \nu \frac {\boldsymbol {\mu}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda \left(\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {\ell_ {q}} ^ {q} - \left(\frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}}\right) ^ {q}\right) + \nu \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2}, \tag {e.13}
$$

now adopting the notation  $\| \pmb{v}\|_{\pmb{\sigma}}^{2} \coloneqq \pmb{v}^{t}\pmb{\sigma}\pmb{v}$  and invoking the assumption  $\pmb{\sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}} = a\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}$ , we rewrite the optimization as follows:

$$
\sup  _ {\lambda \geq 0, \nu} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} \frac {1}{2} \left\| \boldsymbol {u} + \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {h} \right\| _ {\boldsymbol {\sigma}} ^ {2} + \lambda \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {u} \right\| _ {\ell_ {q}} ^ {q} - \left(\frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}}\right) ^ {q}\right) + \nu \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2}, \tag {e.14}
$$

rearranging the terms further we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sup  _ {\lambda \geq 0, \nu} \quad \min  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} \left[ \frac {1}{2} \left\| \boldsymbol {u} + \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {h} \right\| _ {\boldsymbol {\sigma}} ^ {2} + \lambda \left\| \boldsymbol {u} \right\| _ {\ell_ {q}} ^ {q} \right] - \lambda \left(\frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}}\right) ^ {q} + \nu \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2} \\ = \sup  _ {\lambda \geq 0, \nu} e _ {q, \boldsymbol {\sigma}} \left(\boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {h} - \left(\frac {\tau_ {h} \theta}{\alpha} + \frac {\nu}{a}\right) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}}; \lambda\right) - \lambda \left(\frac {\tau_ {h}}{\alpha} \frac {\gamma_ {0}}{\| \boldsymbol {\mu} \| _ {\ell_ {p}}}\right) ^ {q} + \nu \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {h} - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{2}. \tag {e.15} \\ \end{array}
$$

this concludes the proof.

# e.9 proof of lemma b.6

we recall the definition of weighted moreau envelope

$$
e _ {2, \boldsymbol {\sigma}} (\boldsymbol {x}; \lambda) = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v}} \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {x} - \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {\boldsymbol {\sigma}} ^ {2} + \lambda \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2}. \tag {e.16}
$$

setting derivative to zero we get

$$
- \boldsymbol {\sigma} (\boldsymbol {x} - \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*}) + 2 \lambda \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} = 0,
$$

which implies that  $\pmb{v}_{*} = (\pmb{\sigma} + 2\lambda \pmb{i})^{-1}\pmb{\sigma}\pmb{x}$ . now consider a singular value decomposition  $\pmb{\sigma} = \pmb{u}\pmb{s}\pmb{u}^{t}$ . then,  $\pmb{v}_{*} = \pmb{u}(\pmb{s} + 2\lambda \pmb{i})^{-1}\pmb{s}\pmb{u}^{t}\pmb{x}$ . substituting for  $\pmb{v}_{*}$  in (e.16) we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} e _ {2, \boldsymbol {\sigma}} (\boldsymbol {x}; \lambda) = 2 \lambda^ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {u} (\boldsymbol {s} + 2 \lambda \boldsymbol {i}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {s} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {u} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} + \lambda \left\| \boldsymbol {u} (\boldsymbol {s} + 2 \lambda \boldsymbol {i}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {s} \boldsymbol {u} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \\ = \lambda \boldsymbol {x} ^ {t} \boldsymbol {u} ^ {t} (\boldsymbol {s} + 2 \lambda \boldsymbol {i}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {s} \boldsymbol {u} \boldsymbol {x} \\ = \lambda \left\| \boldsymbol {u} (\boldsymbol {s} + 2 \lambda \boldsymbol {i}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {s} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {u} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \\ = \lambda \left\| (\boldsymbol {\sigma} + 2 \lambda \boldsymbol {i}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

which yields the desired result.

# f proposition 3.2 (an extended statement)

consider the adversarial training loss

$$
\mathcal {l} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) := \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(y _ {i} \langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} \rangle - \varepsilon \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {q}}\right),
$$

where the loss  $\ell(t)$  can be expressed as  $\ell(t) = e^{-f(q)}$  obeying the following technical assumptions:

$f:\mathbb{r}\to \mathbb{r}$  is  $c^2$  -smooth.  
$f^{\prime}(q) > 0$  for all  $q\in \mathbb{r}$  
- there exists  $b_f \geq 0$  such that  $qf'(q)$  is non-decreasing for  $q \in (b_f, \infty)$  and  $qf'(q) \to \infty$  as  $q \to \infty$ .  
- let  $g:[f(b_f),\infty)\to [b_f,\infty)$  be the inverse function of  $f$  on the domain  $[b_f,\infty)$ . there exists  $p\geq 0$  such that for all  $x > f(b_{f})$ ,  $y > b_{f}$ ,

$$
\left| \frac {g ^ {\prime \prime} (x)}{g ^ {\prime} (x)} \right| \leq \frac {p}{x}, \quad \left| \frac {f ^ {\prime \prime} (y)}{f ^ {\prime} (y)} \right| \leq \frac {p}{y}.
$$

(it can be verified that the above assumptions are satisfied by exponential loss and logistic loss.) then, the gradient descent iterates

$$
\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\tau + 1} = \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\tau} - \mu \nabla \mathcal {l} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\tau})
$$

with a sufficiently small step size  $\mu$  obey

$$
\lim  _ {t \rightarrow \infty} \left\| \frac {\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t}}{\| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}} - \frac {\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {\varepsilon}}{\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {\varepsilon} \| _ {\ell_ {2}}} \right\| _ {\ell_ {2}} = 0, \tag {f.1}
$$

where  $\widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}^{\varepsilon}$  is the solution to the following max-margin problem

$$
\begin{array}{l} \widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\varepsilon} = \operatorname *{arg  min}_{\boldsymbol {\theta}\in \mathbb{r}^{d}}\quad \| \boldsymbol {\theta}\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2} \\ \text {s u b j e c t} y _ {i} \left\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\rangle - \varepsilon \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {\ell_ {q}} \geq 1. \tag {f.2} \\ \end{array}
$$
